1→#!/usr/bin/env python3
     2→"""
     3→AI Agent Runner with Tool Calling
     4→
     5→This module provides a clean, standalone agent that can execute AI models
     6→with tool calling capabilities. It handles the conversation loop, tool execution,
     7→and response management.
     8→
     9→Features:
    10→- Automatic tool calling loop until completion
    11→- Configurable model parameters
    12→- Error handling and recovery
    13→- Message history management
    14→- Support for multiple model providers
    15→
    16→Usage:
    17→    from run_agent import AIAgent
    18→    
    19→    agent = AIAgent(base_url="http://localhost:30000/v1", model="claude-opus-4-20250514")
    20→    response = agent.run_conversation("Tell me about the latest Python updates")
    21→"""
    22→
    23→import atexit
    24→import collections
    25→import concurrent.futures
    26→import copy

    50→_user_env = _hermes_home / ".env"
    51→_project_env = Path(__file__).parent / '.env'
    52→if _user_env.exists():
    53→    try:
    54→        load_dotenv(dotenv_path=_user_env, encoding="utf-8")
    55→    except UnicodeDecodeError:
    56→        load_dotenv(dotenv_path=_user_env, encoding="latin-1")
    57→    logger.info("Loaded environment variables from %s", _user_env)
    58→elif _project_env.exists():
    59→    try:
    60→        load_dotenv(dotenv_path=_project_env, encoding="utf-8")

    62→        load_dotenv(dotenv_path=_project_env, encoding="latin-1")
    63→    logger.info("Loaded environment variables from %s", _project_env)
    64→else:
    65→    logger.info("No .env file found. Using system environment variables.")

1→"""
     2→Session management for the gateway.
     3→
     4→Handles:
     5→- Session context tracking (where messages come from)
     6→- Session storage (conversations persisted to disk)
     7→- Reset policy evaluation (when to start fresh)
     8→- Dynamic system prompt injection (agent knows its context)
     9→"""
    10→
    11→import logging
    12→import os
    13→import json
    14→import uuid
    15→from pathlib import Path
    16→from datetime import datetime, timedelta
    17→from dataclasses import dataclass
    18→from typing import Dict, List, Optional, Any
    19→
    20→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    21→
    22→from .config import (
    23→    Platform,
    24→    GatewayConfig,
    25→    HomeChannel,
    26→)
    27→
    28→
    29→@dataclass
    30→class SessionSource:
    31→    """
    32→    Describes where a message originated from.
    33→    
    34→    This information is used to:
    35→    1. Route responses back to the right place
    36→    2. Inject context into the system prompt
    37→    3. Track origin for cron job delivery
    38→    """
    39→    platform: Platform
    40→    chat_id: str
    41→    chat_name: Optional[str] = None
    42→    chat_type: str = "dm"  # "dm", "group", "channel", "thread"
    43→    user_id: Optional[str] = None
    44→    user_name: Optional[str] = None
    45→    thread_id: Optional[str] = None  # For forum topics, Discord threads, etc.
    46→    chat_topic: Optional[str] = None  # Channel topic/description (Discord, Slack)
    47→    user_id_alt: Optional[str] = None  # Signal UUID (alternative to phone number)
    48→    chat_id_alt: Optional[str] = None  # Signal group internal ID
    49→    
    50→    @property
    51→    def description(self) -> str:
    52→        """Human-readable description of the source."""
    53→        if self.platform == Platform.LOCAL:
    54→            return "CLI terminal"
    55→        
    56→        parts = []
    57→        if self.chat_type == "dm":
    58→            parts.append(f"DM with {self.user_name or self.user_id or 'user'}")
    59→        elif self.chat_type == "group":
    60→            parts.append(f"group: {self.chat_name or self.chat_id}")
    61→        elif self.chat_type == "channel":
    62→            parts.append(f"channel: {self.chat_name or self.chat_id}")
    63→        else:
    64→            parts.append(self.chat_name or self.chat_id)
    65→        
    66→        if self.thread_id:
    67→            parts.append(f"thread: {self.thread_id}")
    68→        
    69→        return ", ".join(parts)
    70→    
    71→    def to_dict(self) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    72→        d = {
    73→            "platform": self.platform.value,
    74→            "chat_id": self.chat_id,
    75→            "chat_name": self.chat_name,
    76→            "chat_type": self.chat_type,
    77→            "user_id": self.user_id,
    78→            "user_name": self.user_name,
    79→            "thread_id": self.thread_id,
    80→            "chat_topic": self.chat_topic,
    81→        }
    82→        if self.user_id_alt:
    83→            d["user_id_alt"] = self.user_id_alt
    84→        if self.chat_id_alt:
    85→            d["chat_id_alt"] = self.chat_id_alt
    86→        return d
    87→    
    88→    @classmethod
    89→    def from_dict(cls, data: Dict[str, Any]) -> "SessionSource":
    90→        return cls(
    91→            platform=Platform(data["platform"]),
    92→            chat_id=str(data["chat_id"]),
    93→            chat_name=data.get("chat_name"),
    94→            chat_type=data.get("chat_type", "dm"),
    95→            user_id=data.get("user_id"),
    96→            user_name=data.get("user_name"),
    97→            thread_id=data.get("thread_id"),
    98→            chat_topic=data.get("chat_topic"),
    99→            user_id_alt=data.get("user_id_alt"),
   100→            chat_id_alt=data.get("chat_id_alt"),
   101→        )
   102→    
   103→    @classmethod
   104→    def local_cli(cls) -> "SessionSource":
   105→        """Create a source representing the local CLI."""
   106→        return cls(
   107→            platform=Platform.LOCAL,
   108→            chat_id="cli",
   109→            chat_name="CLI terminal",
   110→            chat_type="dm",
   111→        )

   114→@dataclass
   115→class SessionContext:
   116→    """
   117→    Full context for a session, used for dynamic system prompt injection.
   118→    
   119→    The agent receives this information to understand:

   144→            "updated_at": self.updated_at.isoformat() if self.updated_at else None,
   145→        }

1→"""
     2→Delivery routing for cron job outputs and agent responses.
     3→
     4→Routes messages to the appropriate destination based on:
     5→- Explicit targets (e.g., "telegram:123456789")
     6→- Platform home channels (e.g., "telegram" → home channel)
     7→- Origin (back to where the job was created)
     8→- Local (always saved to files)
     9→"""
    10→
    11→import logging
    12→from pathlib import Path
    13→from datetime import datetime
    14→from dataclasses import dataclass
    15→from typing import Dict, List, Optional, Any, Union
    16→
    17→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    18→
    19→MAX_PLATFORM_OUTPUT = 4000
    20→TRUNCATED_VISIBLE = 3800
    21→
    22→from .config import Platform, GatewayConfig
    23→from .session import SessionSource
    24→
    25→
    26→@dataclass
    27→class DeliveryTarget:
    28→    """
    29→    A single delivery target.
    30→    
    31→    Represents where a message should be sent:
    32→    - "origin" → back to source
    33→    - "local" → save to local files
    34→    - "telegram" → Telegram home channel
    35→    - "telegram:123456" → specific Telegram chat
    36→    """
    37→    platform: Platform
    38→    chat_id: Optional[str] = None  # None means use home channel
    39→    thread_id: Optional[str] = None
    40→    is_origin: bool = False
    41→    is_explicit: bool = False  # True if chat_id was explicitly specified
    42→    
    43→    @classmethod
    44→    def parse(cls, target: str, origin: Optional[SessionSource] = None) -> "DeliveryTarget":
    45→        """
    46→        Parse a delivery target string.
    47→        
    48→        Formats:
    49→        - "origin" → back to source
    50→        - "local" → local files only
    51→        - "telegram" → Telegram home channel
    52→        - "telegram:123456" → specific Telegram chat
    53→        """
    54→        target = target.strip().lower()
    55→        
    56→        if target == "origin":
    57→            if origin:
    58→                return cls(
    59→                    platform=origin.platform,
    60→                    chat_id=origin.chat_id,
    61→                    thread_id=origin.thread_id,
    62→                    is_origin=True,
    63→                )
    64→            else:
    65→                # Fallback to local if no origin
    66→                return cls(platform=Platform.LOCAL, is_origin=True)
    67→        
    68→        if target == "local":
    69→            return cls(platform=Platform.LOCAL)
    70→        
    71→        # Check for platform:chat_id format
    72→        if ":" in target:
    73→            platform_str, chat_id = target.split(":", 1)
    74→            try:
    75→                platform = Platform(platform_str)
    76→                return cls(platform=platform, chat_id=chat_id, is_explicit=True)
    77→            except ValueError:
    78→                # Unknown platform, treat as local
    79→                return cls(platform=Platform.LOCAL)
    80→        
    81→        # Just a platform name (use home channel)
    82→        try:
    83→            platform = Platform(target)
    84→            return cls(platform=platform)
    85→        except ValueError:
    86→            # Unknown platform, treat as local
    87→            return cls(platform=Platform.LOCAL)
    88→    
    89→    def to_string(self) -> str:
    90→        """Convert back to string format."""
    91→        if self.is_origin:
    92→            return "origin"
    93→        if self.platform == Platform.LOCAL:
    94→            return "local"
    95→        if self.chat_id:
    96→            return f"{self.platform.value}:{self.chat_id}"
    97→        return self.platform.value
    98→
    99→
   100→class DeliveryRouter:
   101→    """
   102→    Routes messages to appropriate destinations.
   103→    
   104→    Handles the logic of resolving delivery targets and dispatching
   105→    messages to the right platform adapters.
   106→    """
   107→    
   108→    def __init__(self, config: GatewayConfig, adapters: Dict[Platform, Any] = None):
   109→        """
   110→        Initialize the delivery router.
   111→        
   112→        Args:
   113→            config: Gateway configuration
   114→            adapters: Dict mapping platforms to their adapter instances
   115→        """
   116→        self.config = config
   117→        self.adapters = adapters or {}
   118→        self.output_dir = Path.home() / ".hermes" / "cron" / "output"
   119→    
   120→    def resolve_targets(
   121→        self,
   122→        deliver: Union[str, List[str]],
   123→        origin: Optional[SessionSource] = None
   124→    ) -> List[DeliveryTarget]:
   125→        """
   126→        Resolve delivery specification to concrete targets.
   127→        
   128→        Args:
   129→            deliver: Delivery spec - "origin", "telegram", ["local", "discord"], etc.
   130→            origin: The source where the request originated (for "origin" target)
   131→        
   132→        Returns:
   133→            List of resolved delivery targets
   134→        """
   135→        if isinstance(deliver, str):
   136→            deliver = [deliver]
   137→        
   138→        targets = []
   139→        seen_platforms = set()
   140→        
   141→        for target_str in deliver:
   142→            target = DeliveryTarget.parse(target_str, origin)
   143→            
   144→            # Resolve home channel if needed
   145→            if target.chat_id is None and target.platform != Platform.LOCAL:
   146→                home = self.config.get_home_channel(target.platform)
   147→                if home:
   148→                    target.chat_id = home.chat_id
   149→                else:
   150→                    # No home channel configured, skip this platform
   151→                    continue
   152→            
   153→            # Deduplicate
   154→            key = (target.platform, target.chat_id, target.thread_id)
   155→            if key not in seen_platforms:
   156→                seen_platforms.add(key)
   157→                targets.append(target)
   158→        
   159→        # Always include local if configured
   160→        if self.config.always_log_local:
   161→            local_key = (Platform.LOCAL, None)
   162→            if local_key not in seen_platforms:
   163→                targets.append(DeliveryTarget(platform=Platform.LOCAL))
   164→        
   165→        return targets
   166→    
   167→    async def deliver(
   168→        self,
   169→        content: str,
   170→        targets: List[DeliveryTarget],
   171→        job_id: Optional[str] = None,
   172→        job_name: Optional[str] = None,
   173→        metadata: Optional[Dict[str, Any]] = None
   174→    ) -> Dict[str, Any]:
   175→        """
   176→        Deliver content to all specified targets.
   177→        
   178→        Args:
   179→            content: The message/output to deliver
   180→            targets: List of delivery targets
   181→            job_id: Optional job ID (for cron jobs)
   182→            job_name: Optional job name
   183→            metadata: Additional metadata to include
   184→        
   185→        Returns:
   186→            Dict with delivery results per target
   187→        """
   188→        results = {}
   189→        
   190→        for target in targets:
   191→            try:
   192→                if target.platform == Platform.LOCAL:
   193→                    result = self._deliver_local(content, job_id, job_name, metadata)
   194→                else:
   195→                    result = await self._deliver_to_platform(target, content, metadata)
   196→                
   197→                results[target.to_string()] = {
   198→                    "success": True,
   199→                    "result": result
   200→                }
   201→            except Exception as e:
   202→                results[target.to_string()] = {
   203→                    "success": False,
   204→                    "error": str(e)
   205→                }
   206→        
   207→        return results
   208→    
   209→    def _deliver_local(
   210→        self,
   211→        content: str,
   212→        job_id: Optional[str],
   213→        job_name: Optional[str],
   214→        metadata: Optional[Dict[str, Any]]
   215→    ) -> Dict[str, Any]:
   216→        """Save content to local files."""
   217→        timestamp = datetime.now().strftime("%Y%m%d_%H%M%S")
   218→        
   219→        if job_id:
   220→            output_path = self.output_dir / job_id / f"{timestamp}.md"
   221→        else:
   222→            output_path = self.output_dir / "misc" / f"{timestamp}.md"
   223→        
   224→        output_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   225→        
   226→        # Build the output document
   227→        lines = []
   228→        if job_name:
   229→            lines.append(f"# {job_name}")
   230→        else:
   231→            lines.append("# Delivery Output")
   232→        
   233→        lines.append("")
   234→        lines.append(f"**Timestamp:** {datetime.now().strftime('%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S')}")
   235→        
   236→        if job_id:
   237→            lines.append(f"**Job ID:** {job_id}")
   238→        
   239→        if metadata:
   240→            for key, value in metadata.items():
   241→                lines.append(f"**{key}:** {value}")
   242→        
   243→        lines.append("")
   244→        lines.append("---")
   245→        lines.append("")
   246→        lines.append(content)
   247→        
   248→        output_path.write_text("\n".join(lines))
   249→        
   250→        return {
   251→            "path": str(output_path),
   252→            "timestamp": timestamp
   253→        }
   254→    
   255→    def _save_full_output(self, content: str, job_id: str) -> Path:
   256→        """Save full cron output to disk and return the file path."""
   257→        timestamp = datetime.now().strftime("%Y%m%d_%H%M%S")
   258→        out_dir = Path.home() / ".hermes" / "cron" / "output"
   259→        out_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   260→        path = out_dir / f"{job_id}_{timestamp}.txt"
   261→        path.write_text(content)
   262→        return path
   263→
   264→    async def _deliver_to_platform(
   265→        self,
   266→        target: DeliveryTarget,
   267→        content: str,
   268→        metadata: Optional[Dict[str, Any]]
   269→    ) -> Dict[str, Any]:
   270→        """Deliver content to a messaging platform."""
   271→        adapter = self.adapters.get(target.platform)
   272→        
   273→        if not adapter:
   274→            raise ValueError(f"No adapter configured for {target.platform.value}")
   275→        
   276→        if not target.chat_id:
   277→            raise ValueError(f"No chat ID for {target.platform.value} delivery")
   278→        
   279→        # Guard: truncate oversized cron output to stay within platform limits
   280→        if len(content) > MAX_PLATFORM_OUTPUT:
   281→            job_id = (metadata or {}).get("job_id", "unknown")
   282→            saved_path = self._save_full_output(content, job_id)
   283→            logger.info("Cron output truncated (%d chars) — full output: %s", len(content), saved_path)
   284→            content = (
   285→                content[:TRUNCATED_VISIBLE]
   286→                + f"\n\n... [truncated, full output saved to {saved_path}]"
   287→            )
   288→        
   289→        send_metadata = dict(metadata or {})
   290→        if target.thread_id and "thread_id" not in send_metadata:
   291→            send_metadata["thread_id"] = target.thread_id
   292→        return await adapter.send(target.chat_id, content, metadata=send_metadata or None)
   293→
   294→
   295→def parse_deliver_spec(
   296→    deliver: Optional[Union[str, List[str]]],
   297→    origin: Optional[SessionSource] = None,
   298→    default: str = "origin"
   299→) -> Union[str, List[str]]:
   300→    """
   301→    Normalize a delivery specification.
   302→    
   303→    If None or empty, returns the default.
   304→    """
   305→    if not deliver:
   306→        return default
   307→    return deliver
   308→
   309→
   310→def build_delivery_context_for_tool(
   311→    config: GatewayConfig,
   312→    origin: Optional[SessionSource] = None
   313→) -> Dict[str, Any]:
   314→    """
   315→    Build context for the schedule_cronjob tool to understand delivery options.
   316→    
   317→    This is passed to the tool so it can validate and explain delivery targets.
   318→    """
   319→    connected = config.get_connected_platforms()
   320→    
   321→    options = {
   322→        "origin": {
   323→            "description": "Back to where this job was created",
   324→            "available": origin is not None,
   325→        },
   326→        "local": {
   327→            "description": "Save to local files only",
   328→            "available": True,
   329→        }
   330→    }
   331→    
   332→    for platform in connected:
   333→        home = config.get_home_channel(platform)
   334→        options[platform.value] = {
   335→            "description": f"{platform.value.title()} home channel",
   336→            "available": True,
   337→            "home_channel": home.to_dict() if home else None,
   338→        }
   339→    
   340→    return {
   341→        "origin": origin.to_dict() if origin else None,
   342→        "options": options,
   343→        "always_log_local": config.always_log_local,
   344→    }
   345→

    36→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    37→# Image cache utilities
    38→#
    39→# When users send images on messaging platforms, we download them to a local
    40→# cache directory so they can be analyzed by the vision tool (which accepts
    41→# local file paths). This avoids issues with ephemeral platform URLs
    42→# (e.g. Telegram file URLs expire after ~1 hour).
    43→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    44→
    45→# Default location: ~/.hermes/image_cache/
    46→IMAGE_CACHE_DIR = Path(os.path.expanduser("~/.hermes/image_cache"))
    47→
    48→
    49→def get_image_cache_dir() -> Path:
    50→    """Return the image cache directory, creating it if it doesn't exist."""
    51→    IMAGE_CACHE_DIR.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    52→    return IMAGE_CACHE_DIR
    53→
    54→
    55→def cache_image_from_bytes(data: bytes, ext: str = ".jpg") -> str:
    56→    """
    57→    Save raw image bytes to the cache and return the absolute file path.
    58→
    59→    Args:
    60→        data: Raw image bytes.
    61→        ext:  File extension including the dot (e.g. ".jpg", ".png").
    62→
    63→    Returns:
    64→        Absolute path to the cached image file as a string.
    65→    """
    66→    cache_dir = get_image_cache_dir()
    67→    filename = f"img_{uuid.uuid4().hex[:12]}{ext}"
    68→    filepath = cache_dir / filename
    69→    filepath.write_bytes(data)
    70→    return str(filepath)
    71→
    72→
    73→async def cache_image_from_url(url: str, ext: str = ".jpg") -> str:
    74→    """
    75→    Download an image from a URL and save it to the local cache.
    76→
    77→    Uses httpx for async download with a reasonable timeout.
    78→
    79→    Args:
    80→        url: The HTTP/HTTPS URL to download from.
    81→        ext: File extension including the dot (e.g. ".jpg", ".png").
    82→
    83→    Returns:
    84→        Absolute path to the cached image file as a string.
    85→    """
    86→    import httpx
    87→
    88→    async with httpx.AsyncClient(timeout=30.0, follow_redirects=True) as client:
    89→        response = await client.get(
    90→            url,
    91→            headers={
    92→                "User-Agent": "Mozilla/5.0 (compatible; HermesAgent/1.0)",
    93→                "Accept": "image/*,*/*;q=0.8",
    94→            },
    95→        )
    96→        response.raise_for_status()
    97→        return cache_image_from_bytes(response.content, ext)
    98→
    99→
   100→def cleanup_image_cache(max_age_hours: int = 24) -> int:
   101→    """
   102→    Delete cached images older than *max_age_hours*.
   103→
   104→    Returns the number of files removed.
   105→    """
   106→    import time
   107→
   108→    cache_dir = get_image_cache_dir()
   109→    cutoff = time.time() - (max_age_hours * 3600)
   110→    removed = 0
   111→    for f in cache_dir.iterdir():
   112→        if f.is_file() and f.stat().st_mtime < cutoff:
   113→            try:
   114→                f.unlink()
   115→                removed += 1
   116→            except OSError:
   117→                pass
   118→    return removed
   119→
   120→

   403→    def _load_prefill_messages() -> List[Dict[str, Any]]:
   404→        """Load ephemeral prefill messages from config or env var.
   405→        
   406→        Checks HERMES_PREFILL_MESSAGES_FILE env var first, then falls back to
   407→        the prefill_messages_file key in ~/.hermes/config.yaml.
   408→        Relative paths are resolved from ~/.hermes/.
   409→        """
   410→        import json as _json
   411→        file_path = os.getenv("HERMES_PREFILL_MESSAGES_FILE", "")
   412→        if not file_path:
   413→            try:
   414→                import yaml as _y
   415→                cfg_path = _hermes_home / "config.yaml"
   416→                if cfg_path.exists():
   417→                    with open(cfg_path, encoding="utf-8") as _f:
   418→                        cfg = _y.safe_load(_f) or {}
   419→                    file_path = cfg.get("prefill_messages_file", "")
   420→            except Exception:
   421→                pass
   422→        if not file_path:
   423→            return []
   424→        path = Path(file_path).expanduser()
   425→        if not path.is_absolute():
   426→            path = _hermes_home / path
   427→        if not path.exists():
   428→            logger.warning("Prefill messages file not found: %s", path)
   429→            return []
   430→        try:
   431→            with open(path, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
   432→                data = _json.load(f)
   433→            if not isinstance(data, list):
   434→                logger.warning("Prefill messages file must contain a JSON array: %s", path)
   435→                return []
   436→            return data
   437→        except Exception as e:
   438→            logger.warning("Failed to load prefill messages from %s: %s", path, e)
   439→            return []

   466→        Checks HERMES_REASONING_EFFORT env var first, then agent.reasoning_effort
   467→        in config.yaml. Valid: "xhigh", "high", "medium", "low", "minimal", "none".
   468→        Returns None to use default (medium).
   469→        """
   470→        effort = os.getenv("HERMES_REASONING_EFFORT", "")
   471→        if not effort:
   472→            try:
   473→                import yaml as _y

   479→            except Exception:
   480→                pass
   481→        if not effort:

   483→        effort = effort.lower().strip()
   484→        if effort == "none":

   486→        valid = ("xhigh", "high", "medium", "low", "minimal")
   487→        if effort in valid:

   303→    def get_command(self) -> Optional[str]:
   304→        """Extract command name if this is a command message."""
   305→        if not self.is_command():
   306→            return None
   307→        # Split on space and get first word, strip the /
   308→        parts = self.text.split(maxsplit=1)
   309→        return parts[0][1:].lower() if parts else None

   316→        return parts[1] if len(parts) > 1 else ""

   109→}

   111→
   112→class _SafeWriter:
   113→    """Transparent stdout wrapper that catches OSError from broken pipes.
   114→
   115→    When hermes-agent runs as a systemd service, Docker container, or headless
   116→    daemon, the stdout pipe can become unavailable (idle timeout, buffer
   117→    exhaustion, socket reset). Any print() call then raises
   118→    ``OSError: [Errno 5] Input/output error``, which can crash
   119→    run_conversation() — especially via double-fault when the except handler
   120→    also tries to print.
   121→
   122→    This wrapper delegates all writes to the underlying stream and silently
   123→    catches OSError.  It is installed once at the start of run_conversation()
   124→    and is transparent when stdout is healthy (zero overhead on the happy path).
   125→    """
   126→
   127→    __slots__ = ("_inner",)
   128→
   129→    def __init__(self, inner):
   130→        object.__setattr__(self, "_inner", inner)
   131→
   132→    def write(self, data):
   133→        try:
   134→            return self._inner.write(data)
   135→        except OSError:
   136→            return len(data) if isinstance(data, str) else 0
   137→
   138→    def flush(self):
   139→        try:
   140→            self._inner.flush()
   141→        except OSError:
   142→            pass
   143→
   144→    def fileno(self):
   145→        return self._inner.fileno()
   146→
   147→    def isatty(self):
   148→        try:
   149→            return self._inner.isatty()
   150→        except OSError:
   151→            return False
   152→
   153→    def __getattr__(self, name):
   154→        return getattr(self._inner, name)
   155→
   156→
   157→class IterationBudget:
   158→    """Thread-safe shared iteration counter for parent and child agents.
   159→
   160→    Tracks total LLM-call iterations consumed across a parent agent and all
   161→    its subagents.  A single ``IterationBudget`` is created by the parent
   162→    and passed to every child so they share the same cap.
   163→
   164→    ``execute_code`` (programmatic tool calling) iterations are refunded via
   165→    :meth:`refund` so they don't eat into the budget.
   166→    """
   167→
   168→    def __init__(self, max_total: int):
   169→        self.max_total = max_total
   170→        self._used = 0
   171→        self._lock = threading.Lock()
   172→
   173→    def consume(self) -> bool:
   174→        """Try to consume one iteration.  Returns True if allowed."""
   175→        with self._lock:
   176→            if self._used >= self.max_total:
   177→                return False
   178→            self._used += 1
   179→            return True
   180→
   181→    def refund(self) -> None:
   182→        """Give back one iteration (e.g. for execute_code turns)."""
   183→        with self._lock:
   184→            if self._used > 0:
   185→                self._used -= 1
   186→
   187→    @property
   188→    def used(self) -> int:
   189→        return self._used
   190→
   191→    @property
   192→    def remaining(self) -> int:
   193→        with self._lock:
   194→            return max(0, self.max_total - self._used)
   195→
   196→
   197→# Tools that must never run concurrently (interactive / user-facing).
   198→# When any of these appear in a batch, we fall back to sequential execution.
   199→_NEVER_PARALLEL_TOOLS = frozenset({"clarify"})
   200→
   201→# Maximum number of concurrent worker threads for parallel tool execution.
   202→_MAX_TOOL_WORKERS = 8
   203→
   204→
   205→class AIAgent:
   206→    """
   207→    AI Agent with tool calling capabilities.
   208→    
   209→    This class manages the conversation flow, tool execution, and response handling
   210→    for AI models that support function calling.
   211→    """
   212→

1→#!/usr/bin/env python3
     2→"""
     3→SQLite State Store for Hermes Agent.
     4→
     5→Provides persistent session storage with FTS5 full-text search, replacing
     6→the per-session JSONL file approach. Stores session metadata, full message
     7→history, and model configuration for CLI and gateway sessions.
     8→
     9→Key design decisions:
    10→- WAL mode for concurrent readers + one writer (gateway multi-platform)
    11→- FTS5 virtual table for fast text search across all session messages
    12→- Compression-triggered session splitting via parent_session_id chains
    13→- Batch runner and RL trajectories are NOT stored here (separate systems)
    14→- Session source tagging ('cli', 'telegram', 'discord', etc.) for filtering
    15→"""
    16→
    17→import json
    18→import os
    19→import re
    20→import sqlite3
    21→import threading
    22→import time
    23→from pathlib import Path
    24→from typing import Dict, Any, List, Optional
    25→
    26→
    27→DEFAULT_DB_PATH = Path(os.getenv("HERMES_HOME", Path.home() / ".hermes")) / "state.db"
    28→
    29→SCHEMA_VERSION = 4
    30→
    31→SCHEMA_SQL = """
    32→CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS schema_version (
    33→    version INTEGER NOT NULL
    34→);
    35→
    36→CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS sessions (
    37→    id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    38→    source TEXT NOT NULL,
    39→    user_id TEXT,
    40→    model TEXT,
    41→    model_config TEXT,
    42→    system_prompt TEXT,
    43→    parent_session_id TEXT,
    44→    started_at REAL NOT NULL,
    45→    ended_at REAL,
    46→    end_reason TEXT,
    47→    message_count INTEGER DEFAULT 0,
    48→    tool_call_count INTEGER DEFAULT 0,
    49→    input_tokens INTEGER DEFAULT 0,
    50→    output_tokens INTEGER DEFAULT 0,
    51→    title TEXT,
    52→    FOREIGN KEY (parent_session_id) REFERENCES sessions(id)
    53→);
    54→
    55→CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS messages (
    56→    id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
    57→    session_id TEXT NOT NULL REFERENCES sessions(id),
    58→    role TEXT NOT NULL,
    59→    content TEXT,
    60→    tool_call_id TEXT,
    61→    tool_calls TEXT,
    62→    tool_name TEXT,
    63→    timestamp REAL NOT NULL,
    64→    token_count INTEGER,
    65→    finish_reason TEXT
    66→);
    67→
    68→CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_sessions_source ON sessions(source);
    69→CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_sessions_parent ON sessions(parent_session_id);
    70→CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_sessions_started ON sessions(started_at DESC);
    71→CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_messages_session ON messages(session_id, timestamp);
    72→"""
    73→
    74→FTS_SQL = """
    75→CREATE VIRTUAL TABLE IF NOT EXISTS messages_fts USING fts5(
    76→    content,
    77→    content=messages,
    78→    content_rowid=id
    79→);
    80→
    81→CREATE TRIGGER IF NOT EXISTS messages_fts_insert AFTER INSERT ON messages BEGIN
    82→    INSERT INTO messages_fts(rowid, content) VALUES (new.id, new.content);
    83→END;
    84→
    85→CREATE TRIGGER IF NOT EXISTS messages_fts_delete AFTER DELETE ON messages BEGIN
    86→    INSERT INTO messages_fts(messages_fts, rowid, content) VALUES('delete', old.id, old.content);
    87→END;
    88→
    89→CREATE TRIGGER IF NOT EXISTS messages_fts_update AFTER UPDATE ON messages BEGIN
    90→    INSERT INTO messages_fts(messages_fts, rowid, content) VALUES('delete', old.id, old.content);
    91→    INSERT INTO messages_fts(rowid, content) VALUES (new.id, new.content);
    92→END;
    93→"""
    94→
    95→
    96→class SessionDB:
    97→    """
    98→    SQLite-backed session storage with FTS5 search.
    99→
   100→    Thread-safe for the common gateway pattern (multiple reader threads,
   101→    single writer via WAL mode). Each method opens its own cursor.
   102→    """
   103→
   104→    def __init__(self, db_path: Path = None):
   105→        self.db_path = db_path or DEFAULT_DB_PATH
   106→        self.db_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   107→
   108→        self._lock = threading.Lock()
   109→        self._conn = sqlite3.connect(
   110→            str(self.db_path),
   111→            check_same_thread=False,
   112→            timeout=10.0,
   113→        )
   114→        self._conn.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
   115→        self._conn.execute("PRAGMA journal_mode=WAL")
   116→        self._conn.execute("PRAGMA foreign_keys=ON")
   117→
   118→        self._init_schema()
   119→
   120→    def _init_schema(self):
   121→        """Create tables and FTS if they don't exist, run migrations."""
   122→        cursor = self._conn.cursor()
   123→
   124→        cursor.executescript(SCHEMA_SQL)
   125→
   126→        # Check schema version and run migrations
   127→        cursor.execute("SELECT version FROM schema_version LIMIT 1")
   128→        row = cursor.fetchone()
   129→        if row is None:
   130→            cursor.execute("INSERT INTO schema_version (version) VALUES (?)", (SCHEMA_VERSION,))
   131→        else:
   132→            current_version = row["version"] if isinstance(row, sqlite3.Row) else row[0]
   133→            if current_version < 2:
   134→                # v2: add finish_reason column to messages
   135→                try:
   136→                    cursor.execute("ALTER TABLE messages ADD COLUMN finish_reason TEXT")
   137→                except sqlite3.OperationalError:
   138→                    pass  # Column already exists
   139→                cursor.execute("UPDATE schema_version SET version = 2")
   140→            if current_version < 3:
   141→                # v3: add title column to sessions
   142→                try:
   143→                    cursor.execute("ALTER TABLE sessions ADD COLUMN title TEXT")
   144→                except sqlite3.OperationalError:
   145→                    pass  # Column already exists
   146→                cursor.execute("UPDATE schema_version SET version = 3")
   147→            if current_version < 4:
   148→                # v4: add unique index on title (NULLs allowed, only non-NULL must be unique)
   149→                try:
   150→                    cursor.execute(
   151→                        "CREATE UNIQUE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_sessions_title_unique "
   152→                        "ON sessions(title) WHERE title IS NOT NULL"
   153→                    )
   154→                except sqlite3.OperationalError:
   155→                    pass  # Index already exists
   156→                cursor.execute("UPDATE schema_version SET version = 4")
   157→
   158→        # Unique title index — always ensure it exists (safe to run after migrations
   159→        # since the title column is guaranteed to exist at this point)
   160→        try:
   161→            cursor.execute(
   162→                "CREATE UNIQUE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_sessions_title_unique "
   163→                "ON sessions(title) WHERE title IS NOT NULL"
   164→            )
   165→        except sqlite3.OperationalError:
   166→            pass  # Index already exists
   167→
   168→        # FTS5 setup (separate because CREATE VIRTUAL TABLE can't be in executescript with IF NOT EXISTS reliably)
   169→        try:
   170→            cursor.execute("SELECT * FROM messages_fts LIMIT 0")
   171→        except sqlite3.OperationalError:
   172→            cursor.executescript(FTS_SQL)
   173→

## Comprehensive Architecture and Flow Analysis

Based on my thorough exploration of the Hermes Agent codebase, here's a detailed breakdown of the system architecture and how turns/replies work:

---

## 1. PROJECT OVERVIEW

**Hermes Agent** is an AI agent framework that:
- Orchestrates conversations with LLMs (primary support for Claude via OpenRouter)
- Handles tool calling/function calling in an iterative loop
- Supports multiple messaging platforms (Telegram, Discord, Slack, WhatsApp, Signal, Email, HomeAssistant)
- Manages sessions, memory, and conversation state persistence
- Includes a gateway for routing messages from platforms to the agent and responses back

**Key Entry Points:**
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/run_agent.py` - Core AIAgent class with conversation loop
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/gateway/run.py` - Gateway runner orchestrating platform adapters
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/gateway/platforms/base.py` - Base platform adapter interface

---

## 2. ARCHITECTURE LAYERS

### Layer 1: Messaging Platforms (Gateway)
- **Location:** `/gateway/platforms/` (telegram.py, discord.py, slack.py, etc.)
- **Role:** Connect to external messaging services, receive messages, send responses
- **Base Class:** `BasePlatformAdapter` in `/gateway/platforms/base.py`
- **Interface:** All adapters implement `connect()`, `disconnect()`, `send()`, and a message handler callback

### Layer 2: Gateway Runner
- **Location:** `/gateway/run.py` - `GatewayRunner` class
- **Role:** 
  - Manages all platform adapters
  - Routes incoming messages to the agent
  - Handles commands (/new, /reset, /status, etc.)
  - Manages session lifecycle and persistence
  - Implements session hygiene (auto-compression for oversized conversations)

### Layer 3: Session Management
- **Location:** `/gateway/session.py` - `SessionSource`, `SessionContext`, session storage
- **Location:** `/hermes_state.py` - `SessionDB` SQLite-backed storage
- **Role:**
  - Tracks conversation source (platform, user, chat)
  - Stores message history (JSONL + SQLite)
  - Manages session resets and compression
  - Provides FTS5 full-text search

### Layer 4: Agent Core
- **Location:** `/run_agent.py` - `AIAgent` class
- **Role:**
  - Main conversation loop with tool calling
  - API communication with LLM
  - Tool execution and result handling
  - Context compression for large conversations
  - State persistence (trajectory saving)

### Layer 5: Tools & Extensions
- **Location:** `/tools/` - Individual tool implementations
- **Location:** `/model_tools.py` - Tool registry and handler
- **Types:** Terminal execution, file I/O, web tools, vision, memory, cron jobs, etc.

---

## 3. TURN FLOW: "IT'S THE USER'S TURN" TO "REPLY POSTED"

### Step 1: User Sends Message (Platform → Gateway)
```
User (Telegram/Discord/etc.) 
  → Platform receives message
  → Platform adapter calls registered message handler with MessageEvent
```

**MessageEvent Structure** (`/gateway/platforms/base.py:272`):
```python
@dataclass
class MessageEvent:
    text: str
    message_type: MessageType
    source: SessionSource  # platform, user_id, chat_id, etc.
    message_id: str
    media_urls: List[str]  # Images, audio, documents
    media_types: List[str]
```

### Step 2: Gateway Routes Message (GatewayRunner._handle_message)
**Location:** `/gateway/run.py:867`

Key steps:
1. **Authorization check** - Is user allowed?
2. **Priority interrupt** - If an agent is already running, interrupt it with the new message
3. **Command parsing** - Check for `/new`, `/reset`, `/help`, `/model`, etc.
4. **Session management:**
   - Get or create session for this user/chat
   - Load conversation history from SQLite/JSONL
   - Build dynamic session context prompt
5. **Media enrichment:**
   - Auto-analyze images with vision tool
   - Auto-transcribe audio
   - Extract and embed document descriptions
6. **Agent invocation** - Call `_run_agent()` with enriched message

### Step 3: Run Agent (AIAgent.run_conversation)
**Location:** `/run_agent.py:4003`

**Core Loop:**
1. **Initialize:**
   - Add user message to conversation
   - Build system prompt (from cache or freshly)
   - Load conversation history

2. **Main Tool-Calling Loop** (lines 4217-5369):
   ```
   While api_call_count < max_iterations:
       - Make LLM API call with messages + available tools
       - Parse response (may contain tool_calls)
       - If tool calls: execute them, add results to messages, loop
       - If stop/end_turn: extract final_response and break
   ```

3. **Final Response Extraction:**
   - Extract assistant message content
   - Strip `<think>` blocks (internal reasoning)
   - Return result dict with:
     - `final_response` - Text to show user
     - `messages` - Full conversation with all tool calls
     - `api_calls` - Number of iterations
     - `completed` - Whether it finished normally
     - `last_reasoning` - Extracted from thinking tag

### Step 4: Gateway Sends Response Back
**Location:** `/gateway/run.py:1439-1556`

After agent completes:
1. **Update session:**
   - Save new messages to SQLite and JSONL transcript
   - Update token counts and metrics
2. **Return response string** from `_handle_message()`

### Step 5: Platform Adapter Sends to User
**Location:** `/gateway/platforms/base.py:695-760`

When message handler returns:
```python
response = await self._message_handler(event)  # line 706

if response:
    # Extract media and images from response
    media_files, response = self.extract_media(response)
    images, text_content = self.extract_images(response)
    
    # Send text via platform API
    result = await self.send(
        chat_id=event.source.chat_id,
        content=text_content,
        reply_to=event.message_id,
        metadata=_thread_metadata,
    )
    
    # Send images/media
    for image_url in images:
        await self.send_image(chat_id, image_url, ...)
```

---

## 4. DATA MODELS

### Session Record (SQLite - hermes_state.py)
```python
sessions:
  - id: str (PRIMARY KEY, uuid)
  - source: str (cli, telegram, discord, slack, etc.)
  - user_id: str
  - model: str (e.g., "anthropic/claude-opus-4.6")
  - system_prompt: str (snapshot of the prompt used)
  - parent_session_id: str (for compression-triggered splits)
  - started_at: timestamp
  - ended_at: timestamp
  - title: str (optional, user-defined)
  - message_count, tool_call_count, input_tokens, output_tokens
```

### Message Record (SQLite - hermes_state.py)
```python
messages:
  - id: int (PRIMARY KEY, auto-increment)
  - session_id: str (FOREIGN KEY)
  - role: str (system, user, assistant, tool)
  - content: str (message text)
  - tool_call_id: str (if this is a tool result)
  - tool_calls: str (JSON serialized list of tool calls)
  - tool_name: str (if a tool was called)
  - timestamp: float
  - token_count: int
  - finish_reason: str (stop, tool_calls, length, etc.)
```

### Conversation Format (Sent to LLM)
```python
[
    {"role": "system", "content": "...system prompt..."},
    {"role": "user", "content": "user message"},
    {"role": "assistant", "content": "response", "tool_calls": [...]},
    {"role": "tool", "tool_call_id": "...", "content": "result"},
    ...
]
```

---

## 5. LLM INTEGRATION

**Primary Provider:** OpenRouter (supports Claude and other models)

**API Call Flow** (run_agent.py:4377):
```python
response = self._interruptible_api_call(api_kwargs)
```

Where `api_kwargs` includes:
- `model`: Model identifier (e.g., "anthropic/claude-opus-4.6")
- `messages`: Conversation history + system prompt
- `tools`: Available tool definitions (JSON Schema)
- `max_tokens`: Response length limit
- `temperature`, `top_p`: Sampling parameters

**Response Handling:**
1. Check `response.choices[0].finish_reason`:
   - `"stop"` → Extract `message.content` as final response
   - `"tool_calls"` → Parse tool calls, execute, loop again
   - `"length"` → Context limit hit, trigger compression

2. Extract `message.content`:
   - May contain `<think>...</think>` blocks (internal reasoning)
   - Blocks are stripped before sending to user
   - User receives only the final text content

---

## 6. TURN SYSTEM: WHO GOES NEXT?

The system **doesn't have explicit "user's turn vs agent's turn"** detection in the traditional sense. Instead:

**When it's the user's turn to receive:**
1. Agent finishes its main loop (no more tool calls OR max iterations reached)
2. `final_response` is extracted
3. Response is immediately sent back via platform adapter

**When it's the agent's turn to respond:**
1. New message arrives from user
2. Gateway adds it to the message list
3. Agent makes API call with full conversation history
4. Tool loop executes until natural completion

**Interrupt System** (for stopping a running agent):
- If user sends a new message while agent is running, it doesn't wait for the agent to finish
- `_handle_message()` checks `_running_agents` dict (line 915)
- If agent is running for this session, it calls `agent.interrupt(new_message)`
- New message is queued in `_pending_messages`
- Agent's main loop checks `_interrupt_requested` (line 4222) and breaks
- Response generated so far is sent, then on next message the queued message is processed

---

## 7. REPLY POSTING MECHANISM

**The flow is synchronous in the main path:**

```
User Message (async event)
  ↓
Gateway._handle_message() (async)
  ↓
_run_agent() (sync, in thread pool)
  ↓ AIAgent.run_conversation() (sync)
  ↓
return {"final_response": "...", "messages": [...]}
  ↓
Gateway returns response string
  ↓
Platform adapter receives response
  ↓
adapter.send() (async)
  ↓
User receives reply in chat
```

**Non-blocking tool progress:** 
- While agent is running in the thread pool, a separate async task (`send_progress_messages()`) reads from a queue and sends real-time tool execution updates
- Uses message editing (where platform supports it) to keep updates clean
- Only the final response blocks until the thread completes

---

## 8. KEY FILES SUMMARY

| File | Purpose |
|------|---------|
| `/run_agent.py` | Core AI agent with conversation loop and tool calling |
| `/gateway/run.py` | Gateway orchestrator managing all platform adapters |
| `/gateway/platforms/base.py` | Base class for all platform adapters |
| `/gateway/session.py` | Session context and source tracking |
| `/hermes_state.py` | SQLite session and message persistence |
| `/gateway/delivery.py` | Routing for cron job outputs and cross-platform delivery |
| `/agent/prompt_builder.py` | System prompt construction |
| `/agent/context_compressor.py` | Context compression for large conversations |
| `/model_tools.py` | Tool registry and execution handler |
| `/tools/terminal_tool.py`, `/tools/file_tools.py`, etc. | Individual tool implementations |

---

## 9. EXISTING LLM INTEGRATION SUMMARY

**Provider:** OpenAI-compatible API (OpenRouter)

**Model Resolution:**
- Config: `~/.hermes/config.yaml` (model.default)
- Env: `HERMES_MODEL`
- Default: `anthropic/claude-opus-4.6`

**Features:**
- Prefix caching (Anthropic) for multi-turn efficiency
- Reasoning/thinking blocks (`<think>...</think>`)
- Tool calling (function_calling schema)
- Streaming support (for progress updates)
- Multi-provider routing via OpenRouter

**No custom LLM logic** - the system uses standard OpenAI SDK with OpenRouter as the base URL.

   114→@dataclass
   115→class SessionContext:

   118→

   125→    connected_platforms: List[Platform]
   126→    home_channels: Dict[Platform, HomeChannel]
   127→    
   128→    # Session metadata
   129→    session_key: str = ""
   130→    session_id: str = ""
   131→    created_at: Optional[datetime] = None

   133→    
   134→    def to_dict(self) -> Dict[str, Any]:
   135→        return {
   136→            "source": self.source.to_dict(),
   137→            "connected_platforms": [p.value for p in self.connected_platforms],
   138→            "home_channels": {
   139→                p.value: hc.to_dict() for p, hc in self.home_channels.items()
   140→            },
   141→            "[REDACTED],
   142→            "session_id": self.session_id,
   143→            "created_at": self.created_at.isoformat() if self.created_at else None,
   144→            "updated_at": self.updated_at.isoformat() if self.updated_at else None,
   145→        }
   146→
   147→
   148→def build_session_context_prompt(context: SessionContext) -> str:
   149→    """
   150→    Build the dynamic system prompt section that tells the agent about its context.
   151→    
   152→    This is injected into the system prompt so the agent knows:
   153→    - Where messages are coming from
   154→    - What platforms are connected
   155→    - Where it can deliver scheduled task outputs
   156→    """
   157→    lines = [
   158→        "## Current Session Context",
   159→        "",
   160→    ]
   161→    
   162→    # Source info
   163→    platform_name = context.source.platform.value.title()
   164→    if context.source.platform == Platform.LOCAL:
   165→        lines.append(f"**Source:** {platform_name} (the machine running this agent)")
   166→    else:
   167→        lines.append(f"**Source:** {platform_name} ({context.source.description})")
   168→    
   169→    # Channel topic (if available - provides context about the channel's purpose)
   170→    if context.source.chat_topic:
   171→        lines.append(f"**Channel Topic:** {context.source.chat_topic}")
   172→
   173→    # User identity (especially useful for WhatsApp where multiple people DM)
   174→    if context.source.user_name:
   175→        lines.append(f"**User:** {context.source.user_name}")
   176→    elif context.source.user_id:
   177→        lines.append(f"**User ID:** {context.source.user_id}")
   178→    
   179→    # Connected platforms
   180→    platforms_list = ["local (files on this machine)"]
   181→    for p in context.connected_platforms:
   182→        if p != Platform.LOCAL:
   183→            platforms_list.append(f"{p.value}: Connected ✓")
   184→    
   185→    lines.append(f"**Connected Platforms:** {', '.join(platforms_list)}")
   186→    
   187→    # Home channels
   188→    if context.home_channels:
   189→        lines.append("")
   190→        lines.append("**Home Channels (default destinations):**")
   191→        for platform, home in context.home_channels.items():
   192→            lines.append(f"  - {platform.value}: {home.name} (ID: {home.chat_id})")
   193→    
   194→    # Delivery options for scheduled tasks
   195→    lines.append("")
   196→    lines.append("**Delivery options for scheduled tasks:**")
   197→    
   198→    # Origin delivery
   199→    if context.source.platform == Platform.LOCAL:
   200→        lines.append("- `\"origin\"` → Local output (saved to files)")
   201→    else:
   202→        lines.append(f"- `\"origin\"` → Back to this chat ({context.source.chat_name or context.source.chat_id})")
   203→    
   204→    # Local always available
   205→    lines.append("- `\"local\"` → Save to local files only (~/.hermes/cron/output/)")
   206→    
   207→    # Platform home channels
   208→    for platform, home in context.home_channels.items():
   209→        lines.append(f"- `\"{platform.value}\"` → Home channel ({home.name})")
   210→    
   211→    # Note about explicit targeting
   212→    lines.append("")
   213→    lines.append("*For explicit targeting, use `\"platform:chat_id\"` format if the user provides a specific chat ID.*")
   214→    
   215→    return "\n".join(lines)
   216→
   217→
   218→@dataclass
   219→class SessionEntry:
   220→    """
   221→    Entry in the session store.
   222→    
   223→    Maps a session key to its current session ID and metadata.
   224→    """
   225→    session_key: str
   226→    session_id: str
   227→    created_at: datetime
   228→    updated_at: datetime
   229→    
   230→    # Origin metadata for delivery routing
   231→    origin: Optional[SessionSource] = None
   232→    
   233→    # Display metadata
   234→    display_name: Optional[str] = None
   235→    platform: Optional[Platform] = None
   236→    chat_type: str = "dm"
   237→    
   238→    # Token tracking
   239→    input_tokens: int = 0
   240→    output_tokens: int = 0
   241→    total_tokens: int = 0
   242→    
   243→    # Last API-reported prompt tokens (for accurate compression pre-check)
   244→    last_prompt_tokens: int = 0
   245→    
   246→    # Set when a session was created because the previous one expired;
   247→    # consumed once by the message handler to inject a notice into context
   248→    was_auto_reset: bool = False
   249→    
   250→    def to_dict(self) -> Dict[str, Any]:
   251→        result = {
   252→            "[REDACTED],
   253→            "session_id": self.session_id,
   254→            "created_at": self.created_at.isoformat(),
   255→            "updated_at": self.updated_at.isoformat(),
   256→            "display_name": self.display_name,
   257→            "platform": self.platform.value if self.platform else None,
   258→            "chat_type": self.chat_type,
   259→            "input_tokens": self.input_tokens,
   260→            "output_tokens": self.output_tokens,
   261→            "total_tokens": self.total_tokens,
   262→            "last_prompt_tokens": self.last_prompt_tokens,
   263→        }
   264→        if self.origin:
   265→            result["origin"] = self.origin.to_dict()
   266→        return result
   267→    
   268→    @classmethod
   269→    def from_dict(cls, data: Dict[str, Any]) -> "SessionEntry":
   270→        origin = None
   271→        if "origin" in data and data["origin"]:
   272→            origin = SessionSource.from_dict(data["origin"])
   273→        
   274→        platform = None
   275→        if data.get("platform"):
   276→            try:
   277→                platform = Platform(data["platform"])
   278→            except ValueError as e:
   279→                logger.debug("Unknown platform value %r: %s", data["platform"], e)
   280→        
   281→        return cls(
   282→            session_key=data["session_key"],
   283→            session_id=data["session_id"],
   284→            created_at=datetime.fromisoformat(data["created_at"]),
   285→            updated_at=datetime.fromisoformat(data["updated_at"]),
   286→            origin=origin,
   287→            display_name=data.get("display_name"),
   288→            platform=platform,
   289→            chat_type=data.get("chat_type", "dm"),
   290→            input_tokens=data.get("input_tokens", 0),
   291→            output_tokens=data.get("output_tokens", 0),
   292→            total_tokens=data.get("total_tokens", 0),
   293→            last_prompt_tokens=data.get("last_prompt_tokens", 0),
   294→        )
   295→
   296→
   297→def build_session_key(source: SessionSource) -> str:
   298→    """Build a deterministic session key from a message source.
   299→
   300→    This is the single source of truth for session key construction.
   301→
   302→    DM rules:
   303→      - WhatsApp DMs include chat_id (multi-user support).
   304→      - Other DMs include thread_id when present (e.g. Slack threaded DMs),
   305→        so each DM thread gets its own session while top-level DMs share one.
   306→      - Without thread_id or chat_id, all DMs share a single session.
   307→
   308→    Group/channel rules:
   309→      - thread_id differentiates threads within a channel.
   310→      - Without thread_id, all messages in a channel share one session.
   311→    """
   312→    platform = source.platform.value
   313→    if source.chat_type == "dm":
   314→        if source.thread_id:
   315→            return f"agent:main:{platform}:dm:{source.thread_id}"
   316→        if platform == "whatsapp" and source.chat_id:
   317→            return f"agent:main:{platform}:dm:{source.chat_id}"
   318→        return f"agent:main:{platform}:dm"
   319→    if source.thread_id:
   320→        return f"agent:main:{platform}:{source.chat_type}:{source.chat_id}:{source.thread_id}"
   321→    return f"agent:main:{platform}:{source.chat_type}:{source.chat_id}"
   322→
   323→
   324→class SessionStore:
   325→    """
   326→    Manages session storage and retrieval.
   327→    
   328→    Uses SQLite (via SessionDB) for session metadata and message transcripts.
   329→    Falls back to legacy JSONL files if SQLite is unavailable.
   330→    """
   331→    
   332→    def __init__(self, sessions_dir: Path, config: GatewayConfig,
   333→                 has_active_processes_fn=None,
   334→                 on_auto_reset=None):
   335→        self.sessions_dir = sessions_dir
   336→        self.config = config
   337→        self._entries: Dict[str, SessionEntry] = {}
   338→        self._loaded = False
   339→        self._has_active_processes_fn = has_active_processes_fn
   340→        # on_auto_reset is deprecated — memory flush now runs proactively
   341→        # via the background session expiry watcher in GatewayRunner.
   342→        self._pre_flushed_sessions: set = set()  # session_ids already flushed by watcher
   343→        
   344→        # Initialize SQLite session database
   345→        self._db = None
   346→        try:
   347→            from hermes_state import SessionDB
   348→            self._db = SessionDB()
   349→        except Exception as e:
   350→            print(f"[gateway] Warning: SQLite session store unavailable, falling back to JSONL: {e}")
   351→    
   352→    def _ensure_loaded(self) -> None:
   353→        """Load sessions index from disk if not already loaded."""
   354→        if self._loaded:
   355→            return
   356→        
   357→        self.sessions_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   358→        sessions_file = self.sessions_dir / "sessions.json"
   359→        
   360→        if sessions_file.exists():
   361→            try:
   362→                with open(sessions_file, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
   363→                    data = json.load(f)
   364→                    for key, entry_data in data.items():
   365→                        self._entries[key] = SessionEntry.from_dict(entry_data)
   366→            except Exception as e:
   367→                print(f"[gateway] Warning: Failed to load sessions: {e}")
   368→        
   369→        self._loaded = True
   370→    
   371→    def _save(self) -> None:
   372→        """Save sessions index to disk (kept for session key -> ID mapping)."""
   373→        import tempfile
   374→        self.sessions_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   375→        sessions_file = self.sessions_dir / "sessions.json"
   376→
   377→        data = {key: entry.to_dict() for key, entry in self._entries.items()}
   378→        fd, tmp_path = tempfile.mkstemp(
   379→            dir=str(self.sessions_dir), suffix=".tmp", prefix=".sessions_"
   380→        )
   381→        try:
   382→            with os.fdopen(fd, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
   383→                json.dump(data, f, indent=2)
   384→                f.flush()
   385→                os.fsync(f.fileno())
   386→            os.replace(tmp_path, sessions_file)
   387→        except BaseException:
   388→            try:
   389→                os.unlink(tmp_path)
   390→            except OSError as e:
   391→                logger.debug("Could not remove temp file %s: %s", tmp_path, e)
   392→            raise
   393→    
   394→    def _generate_session_key(self, source: SessionSource) -> str:
   395→        """Generate a session key from a source."""
   396→        return build_session_key(source)
   397→    
   398→    def _is_session_expired(self, entry: SessionEntry) -> bool:
   399→        """Check if a session has expired based on its reset policy.
   400→        
   401→        Works from the entry alone — no SessionSource needed.
   402→        Used by the background expiry watcher to proactively flush memories.
   403→        Sessions with active background processes are never considered expired.
   404→        """
   405→        if self._has_active_processes_fn:
   406→            if self._has_active_processes_fn(entry.session_key):
   407→                return False
   408→
   409→        policy = self.config.get_reset_policy(
   410→            platform=entry.platform,
   411→            session_type=entry.chat_type,
   412→        )
   413→
   414→        if policy.mode == "none":
   415→            return False
   416→
   417→        now = datetime.now()
   418→
   419→        if policy.mode in ("idle", "both"):
   420→            idle_deadline = entry.updated_at + timedelta(minutes=policy.idle_minutes)
   421→            if now > idle_deadline:
   422→                return True
   423→
   424→        if policy.mode in ("daily", "both"):
   425→            today_reset = now.replace(
   426→                hour=policy.at_hour,
   427→                minute=0, second=0, microsecond=0,
   428→            )
   429→            if now.hour < policy.at_hour:
   430→                today_reset -= timedelta(days=1)
   431→            if entry.updated_at < today_reset:
   432→                return True
   433→
   434→        return False
   435→
   436→    def _should_reset(self, entry: SessionEntry, source: SessionSource) -> bool:
   437→        """
   438→        Check if a session should be reset based on policy.
   439→        
   440→        Sessions with active background processes are never reset.
   441→        """
   442→        if self._has_active_processes_fn:
   443→            [REDACTED](source)
   444→            if self._has_active_processes_fn(session_key):
   445→                return False
   446→
   447→        policy = self.config.get_reset_policy(
   448→            platform=source.platform,
   449→            session_type=source.chat_type
   450→        )
   451→        
   452→        if policy.mode == "none":
   453→            return False
   454→        
   455→        now = datetime.now()
   456→        
   457→        if policy.mode in ("idle", "both"):
   458→            idle_deadline = entry.updated_at + timedelta(minutes=policy.idle_minutes)
   459→            if now > idle_deadline:
   460→                return True
   461→        
   462→        if policy.mode in ("daily", "both"):
   463→            today_reset = now.replace(
   464→                hour=policy.at_hour, 
   465→                minute=0, 
   466→                second=0, 
   467→                microsecond=0
   468→            )
   469→            if now.hour < policy.at_hour:
   470→                today_reset -= timedelta(days=1)
   471→            
   472→            if entry.updated_at < today_reset:
   473→                return True
   474→        
   475→        return False
   476→    
   477→    def has_any_sessions(self) -> bool:
   478→        """Check if any sessions have ever been created (across all platforms).
   479→
   480→        Uses the SQLite database as the source of truth because it preserves
   481→        historical session records (ended sessions still count).  The in-memory
   482→        ``_entries`` dict replaces entries on reset, so ``len(_entries)`` would
   483→        stay at 1 for single-platform users — which is the bug this fixes.
   484→
   485→        The current session is already in the DB by the time this is called
   486→        (get_or_create_session runs first), so we check ``> 1``.
   487→        """
   488→        if self._db:
   489→            try:
   490→                return self._db.session_count() > 1
   491→            except Exception:
   492→                pass  # fall through to heuristic
   493→        # Fallback: check if sessions.json was loaded with existing data.
   494→        # This covers the rare case where the DB is unavailable.
   495→        self._ensure_loaded()
   496→        return len(self._entries) > 1
   497→    
   498→    def get_or_create_session(
   499→        self, 
   500→        source: SessionSource,
   501→        force_new: bool = False
   502→    ) -> SessionEntry:
   503→        """
   504→        Get an existing session or create a new one.
   505→        
   506→        Evaluates reset policy to determine if the existing session is stale.
   507→        Creates a session record in SQLite when a new session starts.
   508→        """
   509→        self._ensure_loaded()
   510→        
   511→        [REDACTED](source)
   512→        now = datetime.now()
   513→        
   514→        if session_key in self._entries and not force_new:
   515→            entry = self._entries[session_key]
   516→            
   517→            if not self._should_reset(entry, source):
   518→                entry.updated_at = now
   519→                self._save()
   520→                return entry
   521→            else:
   522→                # Session is being auto-reset.  The background expiry watcher
   523→                # should have already flushed memories proactively; discard
   524→                # the marker so it doesn't accumulate.
   525→                was_auto_reset = True
   526→                self._pre_flushed_sessions.discard(entry.session_id)
   527→                if self._db:
   528→                    try:
   529→                        self._db.end_session(entry.session_id, "session_reset")
   530→                    except Exception as e:
   531→                        logger.debug("Session DB operation failed: %s", e)
   532→        else:
   533→            was_auto_reset = False
   534→        
   535→        # Create new session
   536→        session_id = f"{now.strftime('%Y%m%d_%H%M%S')}_{uuid.uuid4().hex[:8]}"
   537→        
   538→        entry = SessionEntry(
   539→            session_key=session_key,
   540→            session_id=session_id,
   541→            created_at=now,
   542→            updated_at=now,
   543→            origin=source,
   544→            display_name=source.chat_name,
   545→            platform=source.platform,
   546→            chat_type=source.chat_type,
   547→            was_auto_reset=was_auto_reset,
   548→        )
   549→        
   550→        self._entries[session_key] = entry
   551→        self._save()
   552→        
   553→        # Create session in SQLite
   554→        if self._db:
   555→            try:
   556→                self._db.create_session(
   557→                    session_id=session_id,
   558→                    source=source.platform.value,
   559→                    user_id=source.user_id,
   560→                )
   561→            except Exception as e:
   562→                print(f"[gateway] Warning: Failed to create SQLite session: {e}")
   563→        
   564→        return entry
   565→    
   566→    def update_session(
   567→        self, 
   568→        session_key: str,
   569→        input_tokens: int = 0,
   570→        output_tokens: int = 0,
   571→        last_prompt_tokens: int = None,
   572→    ) -> None:
   573→        """Update a session's metadata after an interaction."""
   574→        self._ensure_loaded()
   575→        
   576→        if session_key in self._entries:
   577→            entry = self._entries[session_key]
   578→            entry.updated_at = datetime.now()
   579→            entry.input_tokens += input_tokens
   580→            entry.output_tokens += output_tokens
   581→            if last_prompt_tokens is not None:
   582→                entry.last_prompt_tokens = last_prompt_tokens
   583→            entry.total_tokens = entry.input_tokens + entry.output_tokens
   584→            self._save()
   585→            
   586→            if self._db:
   587→                try:
   588→                    self._db.update_token_counts(
   589→                        entry.session_id, input_tokens, output_tokens
   590→                    )
   591→                except Exception as e:
   592→                    logger.debug("Session DB operation failed: %s", e)
   593→    
   594→    def reset_session(self, session_key: str) -> Optional[SessionEntry]:
   595→        """Force reset a session, creating a new session ID."""
   596→        self._ensure_loaded()
   597→        
   598→        if session_key not in self._entries:
   599→            return None
   600→        
   601→        old_entry = self._entries[session_key]
   602→        
   603→        # End old session in SQLite
   604→        if self._db:
   605→            try:
   606→                self._db.end_session(old_entry.session_id, "session_reset")
   607→            except Exception as e:
   608→                logger.debug("Session DB operation failed: %s", e)
   609→        
   610→        now = datetime.now()
   611→        session_id = f"{now.strftime('%Y%m%d_%H%M%S')}_{uuid.uuid4().hex[:8]}"
   612→        
   613→        new_entry = SessionEntry(
   614→            session_key=session_key,
   615→            session_id=session_id,
   616→            created_at=now,
   617→            updated_at=now,
   618→            origin=old_entry.origin,
   619→            display_name=old_entry.display_name,
   620→            platform=old_entry.platform,
   621→            chat_type=old_entry.chat_type,
   622→        )
   623→        
   624→        self._entries[session_key] = new_entry
   625→        self._save()
   626→        
   627→        # Create new session in SQLite
   628→        if self._db:
   629→            try:
   630→                self._db.create_session(
   631→                    session_id=session_id,
   632→                    source=old_entry.platform.value if old_entry.platform else "unknown",
   633→                    user_id=old_entry.origin.user_id if old_entry.origin else None,
   634→                )
   635→            except Exception as e:
   636→                logger.debug("Session DB operation failed: %s", e)
   637→        
   638→        return new_entry
   639→
   640→    def switch_session(self, session_key: str, target_session_id: str) -> Optional[SessionEntry]:
   641→        """Switch a session key to point at an existing session ID.
   642→
   643→        Used by ``/resume`` to restore a previously-named session.
   644→        Ends the current session in SQLite (like reset), but instead of
   645→        generating a fresh session ID, re-uses ``target_session_id`` so the
   646→        old transcript is loaded on the next message.
   647→        """
   648→        self._ensure_loaded()
   649→
   650→        if session_key not in self._entries:
   651→            return None
   652→
   653→        old_entry = self._entries[session_key]
   654→
   655→        # Don't switch if already on that session
   656→        if old_entry.session_id == target_session_id:
   657→            return old_entry
   658→
   659→        # End the current session in SQLite
   660→        if self._db:
   661→            try:
   662→                self._db.end_session(old_entry.session_id, "session_switch")
   663→            except Exception as e:
   664→                logger.debug("Session DB end_session failed: %s", e)
   665→
   666→        now = datetime.now()
   667→        new_entry = SessionEntry(
   668→            session_key=session_key,
   669→            session_id=target_session_id,
   670→            created_at=now,
   671→            updated_at=now,
   672→            origin=old_entry.origin,
   673→            display_name=old_entry.display_name,
   674→            platform=old_entry.platform,
   675→            chat_type=old_entry.chat_type,
   676→        )
   677→
   678→        self._entries[session_key] = new_entry
   679→        self._save()
   680→        return new_entry
   681→
   682→    def list_sessions(self, active_minutes: Optional[int] = None) -> List[SessionEntry]:
   683→        """List all sessions, optionally filtered by activity."""
   684→        self._ensure_loaded()
   685→        
   686→        entries = list(self._entries.values())
   687→        
   688→        if active_minutes is not None:
   689→            cutoff = datetime.now() - timedelta(minutes=active_minutes)
   690→            entries = [e for e in entries if e.updated_at >= cutoff]
   691→        
   692→        entries.sort(key=lambda e: e.updated_at, reverse=True)
   693→        
   694→        return entries
   695→    
   696→    def get_transcript_path(self, session_id: str) -> Path:
   697→        """Get the path to a session's legacy transcript file."""
   698→        return self.sessions_dir / f"{session_id}.jsonl"
   699→    
   700→    def append_to_transcript(self, session_id: str, message: Dict[str, Any], skip_db: bool = False) -> None:
   701→        """Append a message to a session's transcript (SQLite + legacy JSONL).
   702→
   703→        Args:
   704→            skip_db: When True, only write to JSONL and skip the SQLite write.
   705→                     Used when the agent already persisted messages to SQLite
   706→                     via its own _flush_messages_to_session_db(), preventing
   707→                     the duplicate-write bug (#860).
   708→        """
   709→        # Write to SQLite (unless the agent already handled it)
   710→        if self._db and not skip_db:
   711→            try:
   712→                self._db.append_message(
   713→                    session_id=session_id,
   714→                    role=message.get("role", "unknown"),
   715→                    content=message.get("content"),
   716→                    tool_name=message.get("tool_name"),
   717→                    tool_calls=message.get("tool_calls"),
   718→                    tool_call_id=message.get("tool_call_id"),
   719→                )
   720→            except Exception as e:
   721→                logger.debug("Session DB operation failed: %s", e)
   722→        
   723→        # Also write legacy JSONL (keeps existing tooling working during transition)
   724→        transcript_path = self.get_transcript_path(session_id)
   725→        with open(transcript_path, "a", encoding="utf-8") as f:
   726→            f.write(json.dumps(message, ensure_ascii=False) + "\n")
   727→    
   728→    def rewrite_transcript(self, session_id: str, messages: List[Dict[str, Any]]) -> None:
   729→        """Replace the entire transcript for a session with new messages.
   730→        
   731→        Used by /retry, /undo, and /compress to persist modified conversation history.
   732→        Rewrites both SQLite and legacy JSONL storage.
   733→        """
   734→        # SQLite: clear old messages and re-insert
   735→        if self._db:
   736→            try:
   737→                self._db.clear_messages(session_id)
   738→                for msg in messages:
   739→                    self._db.append_message(
   740→                        session_id=session_id,
   741→                        role=msg.get("role", "unknown"),
   742→                        content=msg.get("content"),
   743→                        tool_name=msg.get("tool_name"),
   744→                        tool_calls=msg.get("tool_calls"),
   745→                        tool_call_id=msg.get("tool_call_id"),
   746→                    )
   747→            except Exception as e:
   748→                logger.debug("Failed to rewrite transcript in DB: %s", e)
   749→        
   750→        # JSONL: overwrite the file
   751→        transcript_path = self.get_transcript_path(session_id)
   752→        with open(transcript_path, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
   753→            for msg in messages:
   754→                f.write(json.dumps(msg, ensure_ascii=False) + "\n")
   755→
   756→    def load_transcript(self, session_id: str) -> List[Dict[str, Any]]:
   757→        """Load all messages from a session's transcript."""
   758→        # Try SQLite first
   759→        if self._db:
   760→            try:
   761→                messages = self._db.get_messages_as_conversation(session_id)
   762→                if messages:
   763→                    return messages
   764→            except Exception as e:
   765→                logger.debug("Could not load messages from DB: %s", e)
   766→        
   767→        # Fall back to legacy JSONL
   768→        transcript_path = self.get_transcript_path(session_id)
   769→        
   770→        if not transcript_path.exists():
   771→            return []
   772→        
   773→        messages = []
   774→        with open(transcript_path, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
   775→            for line in f:
   776→                line = line.strip()
   777→                if line:
   778→                    messages.append(json.loads(line))
   779→        
   780→        return messages
   781→
   782→
   783→def build_session_context(
   784→    source: SessionSource,
   785→    config: GatewayConfig,
   786→    session_entry: Optional[SessionEntry] = None
   787→) -> SessionContext:
   788→    """
   789→    Build a full session context from a source and config.
   790→    
   791→    This is used to inject context into the agent's system prompt.
   792→    """
   793→    connected = config.get_connected_platforms()
   794→    
   795→    home_channels = {}
   796→    for platform in connected:
   797→        home = config.get_home_channel(platform)
   798→        if home:
   799→            home_channels[platform] = home
   800→    
   801→    context = SessionContext(
   802→        source=source,
   803→        connected_platforms=connected,
   804→        home_channels=home_channels,
   805→    )
   806→    
   807→    if session_entry:
   808→        context.[REDACTED]
   809→        context.session_id = session_entry.session_id
   810→        context.created_at = session_entry.created_at
   811→        context.updated_at = session_entry.updated_at
   812→    
   813→    return context
   814→
