    36→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    37→# Image cache utilities
    38→#
    39→# When users send images on messaging platforms, we download them to a local
    40→# cache directory so they can be analyzed by the vision tool (which accepts
    41→# local file paths). This avoids issues with ephemeral platform URLs
    42→# (e.g. Telegram file URLs expire after ~1 hour).
    43→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    44→
    45→# Default location: ~/.hermes/image_cache/
    46→IMAGE_CACHE_DIR = Path(os.path.expanduser("~/.hermes/image_cache"))
    47→
    48→
    49→def get_image_cache_dir() -> Path:
    50→    """Return the image cache directory, creating it if it doesn't exist."""
    51→    IMAGE_CACHE_DIR.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    52→    return IMAGE_CACHE_DIR
    53→
    54→
    55→def cache_image_from_bytes(data: bytes, ext: str = ".jpg") -> str:
    56→    """
    57→    Save raw image bytes to the cache and return the absolute file path.
    58→
    59→    Args:
    60→        data: Raw image bytes.
    61→        ext:  File extension including the dot (e.g. ".jpg", ".png").
    62→
    63→    Returns:
    64→        Absolute path to the cached image file as a string.
    65→    """
    66→    cache_dir = get_image_cache_dir()
    67→    filename = f"img_{uuid.uuid4().hex[:12]}{ext}"
    68→    filepath = cache_dir / filename
    69→    filepath.write_bytes(data)
    70→    return str(filepath)
    71→
    72→
    73→async def cache_image_from_url(url: str, ext: str = ".jpg") -> str:
    74→    """
    75→    Download an image from a URL and save it to the local cache.
    76→
    77→    Uses httpx for async download with a reasonable timeout.
    78→
    79→    Args:
    80→        url: The HTTP/HTTPS URL to download from.
    81→        ext: File extension including the dot (e.g. ".jpg", ".png").
    82→
    83→    Returns:
    84→        Absolute path to the cached image file as a string.
    85→    """
    86→    import httpx
    87→
    88→    async with httpx.AsyncClient(timeout=30.0, follow_redirects=True) as client:
    89→        response = await client.get(
    90→            url,
    91→            headers={
    92→                "User-Agent": "Mozilla/5.0 (compatible; HermesAgent/1.0)",
    93→                "Accept": "image/*,*/*;q=0.8",
    94→            },
    95→        )
    96→        response.raise_for_status()
    97→        return cache_image_from_bytes(response.content, ext)

   100→def cleanup_image_cache(max_age_hours: int = 24) -> int:
   101→    """
   102→    Delete cached images older than *max_age_hours*.
   103→
   104→    Returns the number of files removed.
   105→    """
   106→    import time
   107→
   108→    cache_dir = get_image_cache_dir()
   109→    cutoff = time.time() - (max_age_hours * 3600)
   110→    removed = 0
   111→    for f in cache_dir.iterdir():
   112→        if f.is_file() and f.stat().st_mtime < cutoff:
   113→            try:
   114→                f.unlink()
   115→                removed += 1
   116→            except OSError:
   117→                pass
   118→    return removed
   119→

   672→    
   673→    @staticmethod
   674→    def _get_human_delay() -> float:
   675→        """
   676→        Return a random delay in seconds for human-like response pacing.
   677→
   678→        Reads from env vars:
   679→          HERMES_HUMAN_DELAY_MODE: "off" (default) | "natural" | "custom"
   680→          HERMES_HUMAN_DELAY_MIN_MS: minimum delay in ms (default 800, custom mode)
   681→          HERMES_HUMAN_DELAY_MAX_MS: maximum delay in ms (default 2500, custom mode)
   682→        """
   683→        import random
   684→
   685→        mode = os.getenv("HERMES_HUMAN_DELAY_MODE", "off").lower()
   686→        if mode == "off":
   687→            return 0.0
   688→        min_ms = int(os.getenv("HERMES_HUMAN_DELAY_MIN_MS", "800"))
   689→        max_ms = int(os.getenv("HERMES_HUMAN_DELAY_MAX_MS", "2500"))
   690→        if mode == "natural":
   691→            min_ms, max_ms = 800, 2500
   692→        return random.uniform(min_ms / 1000.0, max_ms / 1000.0)
   693→
   694→    async def _process_message_background(self, event: MessageEvent, session_key: str) -> None:
   695→        """Background task that actually processes the message."""
   696→        # Create interrupt event for this session
   697→        interrupt_event = asyncio.Event()
   698→        self._active_sessions[session_key] = interrupt_event
   699→        
   700→        # Start continuous typing indicator (refreshes every 2 seconds)
   701→        _thread_metadata = {"thread_id": event.source.thread_id} if event.source.thread_id else None
   702→        typing_task = asyncio.create_task(self._keep_typing(event.source.chat_id, metadata=_thread_metadata))
   703→        
   704→        try:
   705→            # Call the handler (this can take a while with tool calls)
   706→            response = await self._message_handler(event)
   707→            
   708→            # Send response if any
   709→            if not response:
   710→                logger.warning("[%s] Handler returned empty/None response for %s", self.name, event.source.chat_id)

   712→                # Extract MEDIA:<path> tags (from TTS tool) before other processing

   720→                # Send the text portion first (if any remains after extractions)
   721→                if text_content:

   742→

1→"""
     2→Delivery routing for cron job outputs and agent responses.
     3→
     4→Routes messages to the appropriate destination based on:
     5→- Explicit targets (e.g., "telegram:123456789")
     6→- Platform home channels (e.g., "telegram" → home channel)
     7→- Origin (back to where the job was created)
     8→- Local (always saved to files)
     9→"""
    10→
    11→import logging
    12→from pathlib import Path
    13→from datetime import datetime
    14→from dataclasses import dataclass
    15→from typing import Dict, List, Optional, Any, Union
    16→
    17→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    18→
    19→MAX_PLATFORM_OUTPUT = 4000
    20→TRUNCATED_VISIBLE = 3800
    21→
    22→from .config import Platform, GatewayConfig
    23→from .session import SessionSource
    24→
    25→
    26→@dataclass
    27→class DeliveryTarget:
    28→    """
    29→    A single delivery target.
    30→    
    31→    Represents where a message should be sent:
    32→    - "origin" → back to source
    33→    - "local" → save to local files
    34→    - "telegram" → Telegram home channel
    35→    - "telegram:123456" → specific Telegram chat
    36→    """
    37→    platform: Platform
    38→    chat_id: Optional[str] = None  # None means use home channel
    39→    thread_id: Optional[str] = None
    40→    is_origin: bool = False
    41→    is_explicit: bool = False  # True if chat_id was explicitly specified
    42→    
    43→    @classmethod
    44→    def parse(cls, target: str, origin: Optional[SessionSource] = None) -> "DeliveryTarget":
    45→        """
    46→        Parse a delivery target string.
    47→        
    48→        Formats:
    49→        - "origin" → back to source
    50→        - "local" → local files only
    51→        - "telegram" → Telegram home channel
    52→        - "telegram:123456" → specific Telegram chat
    53→        """
    54→        target = target.strip().lower()
    55→        
    56→        if target == "origin":
    57→            if origin:
    58→                return cls(
    59→                    platform=origin.platform,
    60→                    chat_id=origin.chat_id,
    61→                    thread_id=origin.thread_id,
    62→                    is_origin=True,
    63→                )
    64→            else:
    65→                # Fallback to local if no origin
    66→                return cls(platform=Platform.LOCAL, is_origin=True)
    67→        
    68→        if target == "local":
    69→            return cls(platform=Platform.LOCAL)
    70→        
    71→        # Check for platform:chat_id format
    72→        if ":" in target:
    73→            platform_str, chat_id = target.split(":", 1)
    74→            try:
    75→                platform = Platform(platform_str)
    76→                return cls(platform=platform, chat_id=chat_id, is_explicit=True)
    77→            except ValueError:
    78→                # Unknown platform, treat as local
    79→                return cls(platform=Platform.LOCAL)
    80→        
    81→        # Just a platform name (use home channel)
    82→        try:
    83→            platform = Platform(target)
    84→            return cls(platform=platform)
    85→        except ValueError:
    86→            # Unknown platform, treat as local
    87→            return cls(platform=Platform.LOCAL)
    88→    
    89→    def to_string(self) -> str:
    90→        """Convert back to string format."""
    91→        if self.is_origin:
    92→            return "origin"
    93→        if self.platform == Platform.LOCAL:
    94→            return "local"
    95→        if self.chat_id:
    96→            return f"{self.platform.value}:{self.chat_id}"
    97→        return self.platform.value
    98→
    99→
   100→class DeliveryRouter:
   101→    """
   102→    Routes messages to appropriate destinations.
   103→    
   104→    Handles the logic of resolving delivery targets and dispatching
   105→    messages to the right platform adapters.
   106→    """
   107→    
   108→    def __init__(self, config: GatewayConfig, adapters: Dict[Platform, Any] = None):
   109→        """
   110→        Initialize the delivery router.
   111→        
   112→        Args:
   113→            config: Gateway configuration
   114→            adapters: Dict mapping platforms to their adapter instances
   115→        """
   116→        self.config = config
   117→        self.adapters = adapters or {}
   118→        self.output_dir = Path.home() / ".hermes" / "cron" / "output"
   119→    
   120→    def resolve_targets(
   121→        self,
   122→        deliver: Union[str, List[str]],
   123→        origin: Optional[SessionSource] = None
   124→    ) -> List[DeliveryTarget]:
   125→        """
   126→        Resolve delivery specification to concrete targets.
   127→        
   128→        Args:
   129→            deliver: Delivery spec - "origin", "telegram", ["local", "discord"], etc.
   130→            origin: The source where the request originated (for "origin" target)
   131→        
   132→        Returns:
   133→            List of resolved delivery targets
   134→        """
   135→        if isinstance(deliver, str):
   136→            deliver = [deliver]
   137→        
   138→        targets = []
   139→        seen_platforms = set()
   140→        
   141→        for target_str in deliver:
   142→            target = DeliveryTarget.parse(target_str, origin)
   143→            
   144→            # Resolve home channel if needed
   145→            if target.chat_id is None and target.platform != Platform.LOCAL:
   146→                home = self.config.get_home_channel(target.platform)
   147→                if home:
   148→                    target.chat_id = home.chat_id
   149→                else:
   150→                    # No home channel configured, skip this platform
   151→                    continue
   152→            
   153→            # Deduplicate
   154→            key = (target.platform, target.chat_id, target.thread_id)
   155→            if key not in seen_platforms:
   156→                seen_platforms.add(key)
   157→                targets.append(target)
   158→        
   159→        # Always include local if configured
   160→        if self.config.always_log_local:
   161→            local_key = (Platform.LOCAL, None)
   162→            if local_key not in seen_platforms:
   163→                targets.append(DeliveryTarget(platform=Platform.LOCAL))
   164→        
   165→        return targets
   166→    
   167→    async def deliver(
   168→        self,
   169→        content: str,
   170→        targets: List[DeliveryTarget],
   171→        job_id: Optional[str] = None,
   172→        job_name: Optional[str] = None,
   173→        metadata: Optional[Dict[str, Any]] = None
   174→    ) -> Dict[str, Any]:
   175→        """
   176→        Deliver content to all specified targets.
   177→        
   178→        Args:
   179→            content: The message/output to deliver
   180→            targets: List of delivery targets
   181→            job_id: Optional job ID (for cron jobs)
   182→            job_name: Optional job name
   183→            metadata: Additional metadata to include
   184→        
   185→        Returns:
   186→            Dict with delivery results per target
   187→        """
   188→        results = {}
   189→        
   190→        for target in targets:
   191→            try:
   192→                if target.platform == Platform.LOCAL:
   193→                    result = self._deliver_local(content, job_id, job_name, metadata)
   194→                else:
   195→                    result = await self._deliver_to_platform(target, content, metadata)
   196→                
   197→                results[target.to_string()] = {
   198→                    "success": True,
   199→                    "result": result
   200→                }
   201→            except Exception as e:
   202→                results[target.to_string()] = {
   203→                    "success": False,
   204→                    "error": str(e)
   205→                }
   206→        
   207→        return results
   208→    
   209→    def _deliver_local(
   210→        self,
   211→        content: str,
   212→        job_id: Optional[str],
   213→        job_name: Optional[str],
   214→        metadata: Optional[Dict[str, Any]]
   215→    ) -> Dict[str, Any]:
   216→        """Save content to local files."""
   217→        timestamp = datetime.now().strftime("%Y%m%d_%H%M%S")
   218→        
   219→        if job_id:
   220→            output_path = self.output_dir / job_id / f"{timestamp}.md"
   221→        else:
   222→            output_path = self.output_dir / "misc" / f"{timestamp}.md"
   223→        
   224→        output_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   225→        
   226→        # Build the output document
   227→        lines = []
   228→        if job_name:
   229→            lines.append(f"# {job_name}")
   230→        else:
   231→            lines.append("# Delivery Output")
   232→        
   233→        lines.append("")
   234→        lines.append(f"**Timestamp:** {datetime.now().strftime('%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S')}")
   235→        
   236→        if job_id:
   237→            lines.append(f"**Job ID:** {job_id}")
   238→        
   239→        if metadata:
   240→            for key, value in metadata.items():
   241→                lines.append(f"**{key}:** {value}")
   242→        
   243→        lines.append("")
   244→        lines.append("---")
   245→        lines.append("")
   246→        lines.append(content)
   247→        
   248→        output_path.write_text("\n".join(lines))
   249→        
   250→        return {
   251→            "path": str(output_path),
   252→            "timestamp": timestamp
   253→        }
   254→    
   255→    def _save_full_output(self, content: str, job_id: str) -> Path:
   256→        """Save full cron output to disk and return the file path."""
   257→        timestamp = datetime.now().strftime("%Y%m%d_%H%M%S")
   258→        out_dir = Path.home() / ".hermes" / "cron" / "output"
   259→        out_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   260→        path = out_dir / f"{job_id}_{timestamp}.txt"
   261→        path.write_text(content)
   262→        return path
   263→
   264→    async def _deliver_to_platform(
   265→        self,
   266→        target: DeliveryTarget,
   267→        content: str,
   268→        metadata: Optional[Dict[str, Any]]
   269→    ) -> Dict[str, Any]:
   270→        """Deliver content to a messaging platform."""
   271→        adapter = self.adapters.get(target.platform)
   272→        
   273→        if not adapter:
   274→            raise ValueError(f"No adapter configured for {target.platform.value}")
   275→        
   276→        if not target.chat_id:
   277→            raise ValueError(f"No chat ID for {target.platform.value} delivery")
   278→        
   279→        # Guard: truncate oversized cron output to stay within platform limits
   280→        if len(content) > MAX_PLATFORM_OUTPUT:
   281→            job_id = (metadata or {}).get("job_id", "unknown")
   282→            saved_path = self._save_full_output(content, job_id)
   283→            logger.info("Cron output truncated (%d chars) — full output: %s", len(content), saved_path)
   284→            content = (
   285→                content[:TRUNCATED_VISIBLE]
   286→                + f"\n\n... [truncated, full output saved to {saved_path}]"
   287→            )
   288→        
   289→        send_metadata = dict(metadata or {})
   290→        if target.thread_id and "thread_id" not in send_metadata:
   291→            send_metadata["thread_id"] = target.thread_id
   292→        return await adapter.send(target.chat_id, content, metadata=send_metadata or None)
   293→
   294→
   295→def parse_deliver_spec(
   296→    deliver: Optional[Union[str, List[str]]],
   297→    origin: Optional[SessionSource] = None,
   298→    default: str = "origin"
   299→) -> Union[str, List[str]]:
   300→    """
   301→    Normalize a delivery specification.
   302→    
   303→    If None or empty, returns the default.
   304→    """
   305→    if not deliver:
   306→        return default
   307→    return deliver
   308→
   309→
   310→def build_delivery_context_for_tool(
   311→    config: GatewayConfig,
   312→    origin: Optional[SessionSource] = None
   313→) -> Dict[str, Any]:
   314→    """
   315→    Build context for the schedule_cronjob tool to understand delivery options.
   316→    
   317→    This is passed to the tool so it can validate and explain delivery targets.
   318→    """
   319→    connected = config.get_connected_platforms()
   320→    
   321→    options = {
   322→        "origin": {
   323→            "description": "Back to where this job was created",
   324→            "available": origin is not None,
   325→        },
   326→        "local": {
   327→            "description": "Save to local files only",
   328→            "available": True,
   329→        }
   330→    }
   331→    
   332→    for platform in connected:
   333→        home = config.get_home_channel(platform)
   334→        options[platform.value] = {
   335→            "description": f"{platform.value.title()} home channel",
   336→            "available": True,
   337→            "home_channel": home.to_dict() if home else None,
   338→        }
   339→    
   340→    return {
   341→        "origin": origin.to_dict() if origin else None,
   342→        "options": options,
   343→        "always_log_local": config.always_log_local,
   344→    }
   345→

   677→    
   678→    async def _session_expiry_watcher(self, interval: int = 300):
   679→        """Background task that proactively flushes memories for expired sessions.
   680→        
   681→        Runs every `interval` seconds (default 5 min).  For each session that
   682→        has expired according to its reset policy, flushes memories in a thread
   683→        pool and marks the session so it won't be flushed again.
   684→
   685→        This means memories are already saved by the time the user sends their
   686→        next message, so there's no blocking delay.
   687→        """
   688→        await asyncio.sleep(60)  # initial delay — let the gateway fully start
   689→        while self._running:
   690→            try:
   691→                self.session_store._ensure_loaded()
   692→                for key, entry in list(self.session_store._entries.items()):
   693→                    if entry.session_id in self.session_store._pre_flushed_sessions:
   694→                        continue  # already flushed this session
   695→                    if not self.session_store._is_session_expired(entry):
   696→                        continue  # session still active
   697→                    # Session has expired — flush memories in the background
   698→                    logger.info(
   699→                        "Session %s expired (key=%s), flushing memories proactively",
   700→                        entry.session_id, key,
   701→                    )
   702→                    try:
   703→                        await self._async_flush_memories(entry.session_id)
   704→                        self._shutdown_gateway_honcho(key)
   705→                        self.session_store._pre_flushed_sessions.add(entry.session_id)
   706→                    except Exception as e:
   707→                        logger.debug("Proactive memory flush failed for %s: %s", entry.session_id, e)
   708→            except Exception as e:
   709→                logger.debug("Session expiry watcher error: %s", e)
   710→            # Sleep in small increments so we can stop quickly
   711→            for _ in range(interval):
   712→                if not self._running:
   713→                    break
   714→                await asyncio.sleep(1)
   715→
   716→    async def stop(self) -> None:
   717→        """Stop the gateway and disconnect all adapters."""
   718→        logger.info("Stopping gateway...")
   719→        self._running = False
   720→        
   721→        for platform, adapter in self.adapters.items():
   722→            try:
   723→                await adapter.disconnect()
   724→                logger.info("✓ %s disconnected", platform.value)
   725→            except Exception as e:
   726→                logger.error("✗ %s disconnect error: %s", platform.value, e)
   727→
   728→        self.adapters.clear()
   729→        self._shutdown_all_gateway_honcho()
   730→        self._shutdown_event.set()
   731→        
   732→        from gateway.status import remove_pid_file
   733→        remove_pid_file()
   734→        
   735→        logger.info("Gateway stopped")
   736→    
   737→    async def wait_for_shutdown(self) -> None:
   738→        """Wait for shutdown signal."""
   739→        await self._shutdown_event.wait()
   740→

   798→    def _is_user_authorized(self, source: SessionSource) -> bool:
   799→        """
   800→        Check if a user is authorized to use the bot.
   801→        
   802→        Checks in order:
   803→        1. Per-platform allow-all flag (e.g., DISCORD_ALLOW_ALL_USERS=true)
   804→        2. Environment variable allowlists (TELEGRAM_ALLOWED_USERS, etc.)
   805→        3. DM pairing approved list
   806→        4. Global allow-all (GATEWAY_ALLOW_ALL_USERS=true)
   807→        5. Default: deny
   808→        """
   809→        # Home Assistant events are system-generated (state changes), not
   810→        # user-initiated messages.  The HASS_TOKEN already authenticates the
   811→        # connection, so HA events are always authorized.
   812→        if source.platform == Platform.HOMEASSISTANT:
   813→            return True
   814→
   815→        user_id = source.user_id
   816→        if not user_id:
   817→            return False
   818→
   819→        platform_env_map = {
   820→            Platform.TELEGRAM: "TELEGRAM_ALLOWED_USERS",
   821→            Platform.DISCORD: "DISCORD_ALLOWED_USERS",
   822→            Platform.WHATSAPP: "WHATSAPP_ALLOWED_USERS",
   823→            Platform.SLACK: "SLACK_ALLOWED_USERS",
   824→            Platform.SIGNAL: "SIGNAL_ALLOWED_USERS",
   825→            Platform.EMAIL: "EMAIL_ALLOWED_USERS",
   826→        }
   827→        platform_allow_all_map = {
   828→            Platform.TELEGRAM: "TELEGRAM_ALLOW_ALL_USERS",
   829→            Platform.DISCORD: "DISCORD_ALLOW_ALL_USERS",
   830→            Platform.WHATSAPP: "WHATSAPP_ALLOW_ALL_USERS",
   831→            Platform.SLACK: "SLACK_ALLOW_ALL_USERS",
   832→            Platform.SIGNAL: "SIGNAL_ALLOW_ALL_USERS",
   833→            Platform.EMAIL: "EMAIL_ALLOW_ALL_USERS",
   834→        }
   835→
   836→        # Per-platform allow-all flag (e.g., DISCORD_ALLOW_ALL_USERS=true)
   837→        platform_allow_all_var = platform_allow_all_map.get(source.platform, "")
   838→        if platform_allow_all_var and os.getenv(platform_allow_all_var, "").lower() in ("true", "1", "yes"):
   839→            return True
   840→
   841→        # Check pairing store (always checked, regardless of allowlists)
   842→        platform_name = source.platform.value if source.platform else ""
   843→        if self.pairing_store.is_approved(platform_name, user_id):
   844→            return True
   845→
   846→        # Check platform-specific and global allowlists
   847→        platform_allowlist = os.getenv(platform_env_map.get(source.platform, ""), "").strip()
   848→        global_allowlist = os.getenv("GATEWAY_ALLOWED_USERS", "").strip()
   849→
   850→        if not platform_allowlist and not global_allowlist:
   851→            # No allowlists configured -- check global allow-all flag
   852→            return os.getenv("GATEWAY_ALLOW_ALL_USERS", "").lower() in ("true", "1", "yes")
   853→
   854→        # Check if user is in any allowlist
   855→        allowed_ids = set()
   856→        if platform_allowlist:
   857→            allowed_ids.update(uid.strip() for uid in platform_allowlist.split(",") if uid.strip())
   858→        if global_allowlist:
   859→            allowed_ids.update(uid.strip() for uid in global_allowlist.split(",") if uid.strip())
   860→
   861→        # WhatsApp JIDs have @s.whatsapp.net suffix — strip it for comparison
   862→        check_ids = {user_id}
   863→        if "@" in user_id:
   864→            check_ids.add(user_id.split("@")[0])
   865→        return bool(check_ids & allowed_ids)
   866→    
   867→    async def _handle_message(self, event: MessageEvent) -> Optional[str]:
   868→        """
   869→        Handle an incoming message from any platform.
   870→        
   871→        This is the core message processing pipeline:
   872→        1. Check user authorization
   873→        2. Check for commands (/new, /reset, etc.)
   874→        3. Check for running agent and interrupt if needed
   875→        4. Get or create session
   876→        5. Build context for agent
   877→        6. Run agent conversation
   878→        7. Return response
   879→        """
   880→        source = event.source
   881→        
   882→        # Check if user is authorized
   883→        if not self._is_user_authorized(source):
   884→            logger.warning("Unauthorized user: %s (%s) on %s", source.user_id, source.user_name, source.platform.value)
   885→            # In DMs: offer pairing code. In groups: silently ignore.
   886→            if source.chat_type == "dm":
   887→                platform_name = source.platform.value if source.platform else "unknown"
   888→                code = self.pairing_store.generate_code(
   889→                    platform_name, source.user_id, source.user_name or ""
   890→                )
   891→                if code:
   892→                    adapter = self.adapters.get(source.platform)
   893→                    if adapter:
   894→                        await adapter.send(
   895→                            source.chat_id,
   896→                            f"Hi~ I don't recognize you yet!\n\n"
   897→                            f"Here's your pairing code: `{code}`\n\n"
   898→                            f"Ask the bot owner to run:\n"
   899→                            f"`hermes pairing approve {platform_name} {code}`"
```

  1949→    async def _handle_retry_command(self, event: MessageEvent) -> str:
  1950→        """Handle /retry command - re-send the last user message."""
  1951→        source = event.source
  1952→        session_entry = self.session_store.get_or_create_session(source)
  1953→        history = self.session_store.load_transcript(session_entry.session_id)
  1954→        
  1955→        # Find the last user message
  1956→        last_user_msg = None
  1957→        last_user_idx = None
  1958→        for i in range(len(history) - 1, -1, -1):
  1959→            if history[i].get("role") == "user":
  1960→                last_user_msg = history[i].get("content", "")
  1961→                last_user_idx = i
  1962→                break
  1963→        
  1964→        if not last_user_msg:
  1965→            return "No previous message to retry."
  1966→        
  1967→        # Truncate history to before the last user message and persist
  1968→        truncated = history[:last_user_idx]
  1969→        self.session_store.rewrite_transcript(session_entry.session_id, truncated)
  1970→        # Reset stored token count — transcript was truncated
  1971→        session_entry.last_prompt_tokens = 0
  1972→        
  1973→        # Re-send by creating a fake text event with the old message
  1974→        retry_event = MessageEvent(
  1975→            text=last_user_msg,
  1976→            message_type=MessageType.TEXT,
  1977→            source=source,
  1978→            raw_message=event.raw_message,
  1979→        )
  1980→        
  1981→        # Let the normal message handler process it
  1982→        return await self._handle_message(retry_event)
  1983→

  1985→        """Handle /undo command - remove the last user/assistant exchange."""

  1988→        history = self.session_store.load_transcript(session_entry.session_id)
  1989→        
  1990→        # Find the last user message and remove everything from it onward
  1991→        last_user_idx = None
  1992→        for i in range(len(history) - 1, -1, -1):
  1993→            if history[i].get("role") == "user":
  1994→                last_user_idx = i
  1995→                break
  1996→        
  1997→        if last_user_idx is None:
  1998→            return "Nothing to undo."
  1999→        
  2000→        removed_msg = history[last_user_idx].get("content", "")
  2001→        removed_count = len(history) - last_user_idx
  2002→        self.session_store.rewrite_transcript(session_entry.session_id, history[:last_user_idx])
  2003→        # Reset stored token count — transcript was truncated
  2004→        session_entry.last_prompt_tokens = 0
  2005→        
  2006→        preview = removed_msg[:40] + "..." if len(removed_msg) > 40 else removed_msg
  2007→        return f"↩️ Undid {removed_count} message(s).\nRemoved: \"{preview}\""

1→"""
     2→Cron job management tools for Hermes Agent.
     3→
     4→These tools allow the agent to schedule, list, and remove automated tasks.
     5→Only available when running via CLI (hermes-cli toolset).
     6→
     7→IMPORTANT: Cronjobs run in isolated sessions with NO prior context.
     8→The prompt must contain ALL necessary information.
     9→"""
    10→
    11→import json
    12→import os
    13→import re
    14→from typing import Optional
    15→
    16→# Import from cron module (will be available when properly installed)
    17→import sys
    18→from pathlib import Path
    19→sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).parent.parent))
    20→
    21→from cron.jobs import create_job, get_job, list_jobs, remove_job
    22→
    23→
    24→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    25→# Cron prompt scanning — critical-severity patterns only, since cron prompts
    26→# run in fresh sessions with full tool access.
    27→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    28→
    29→_CRON_THREAT_PATTERNS = [
    30→    (r'ignore\s+(?:\w+\s+)*(?:previous|all|above|prior)\s+(?:\w+\s+)*instructions', "prompt_injection"),
    31→    (r'do\s+not\s+tell\s+the\s+user', "deception_hide"),
    32→    (r'system\s+prompt\s+override', "sys_prompt_override"),
    33→    (r'disregard\s+(your|all|any)\s+(instructions|rules|guidelines)', "disregard_rules"),
    34→    (r'curl\s+[^\n]*\$\{?\w*(KEY|TOKEN|SECRET|PASSWORD|CREDENTIAL|API)', "exfil_curl"),
    35→    (r'wget\s+[^\n]*\$\{?\w*(KEY|TOKEN|SECRET|PASSWORD|CREDENTIAL|API)', "exfil_wget"),
    36→    (r'cat\s+[^\n]*(\.env|credentials|\.netrc|\.pgpass)', "read_secrets"),
    37→    (r'authorized_keys', "ssh_backdoor"),
    38→    (r'/etc/sudoers|visudo', "sudoers_mod"),
    39→    (r'rm\s+-rf\s+/', "destructive_root_rm"),
    40→]
    41→
    42→_CRON_INVISIBLE_CHARS = {
    43→    '\u200b', '\u200c', '\u200d', '\u2060', '\ufeff',
    44→    '\u202a', '\u202b', '\u202c', '\u202d', '\u202e',
    45→}
    46→
    47→
    48→def _scan_cron_prompt(prompt: str) -> str:
    49→    """Scan a cron prompt for critical threats. Returns error string if blocked, else empty."""
    50→    for char in _CRON_INVISIBLE_CHARS:
    51→        if char in prompt:
    52→            return f"Blocked: prompt contains invisible unicode U+{ord(char):04X} (possible injection)."
    53→    for pattern, pid in _CRON_THREAT_PATTERNS:
    54→        if re.search(pattern, prompt, re.IGNORECASE):
    55→            return f"Blocked: prompt matches threat pattern '{pid}'. Cron prompts must not contain injection or exfiltration payloads."
    56→    return ""
    57→
    58→
    59→# =============================================================================
    60→# Tool: schedule_cronjob
    61→# =============================================================================
    62→
    63→def schedule_cronjob(
    64→    prompt: str,
    65→    schedule: str,
    66→    name: Optional[str] = None,
    67→    repeat: Optional[int] = None,
    68→    deliver: Optional[str] = None,
    69→    task_id: str = None
    70→) -> str:
    71→    """
    72→    Schedule an automated task to run the agent on a schedule.
    73→    
    74→    IMPORTANT: When the cronjob runs, it starts a COMPLETELY FRESH session.
    75→    The agent will have NO memory of this conversation or any prior context.
    76→    Therefore, the prompt MUST contain ALL necessary information:
    77→    - Full context of what needs to be done
    78→    - Specific file paths, URLs, or identifiers
    79→    - Clear success criteria
    80→    - Any relevant background information
    81→    
    82→    BAD prompt:  "Check on that server issue"
    83→    GOOD prompt: "SSH into server 192.168.1.100 as user 'deploy', check if nginx 
    84→                  is running with 'systemctl status nginx', and verify the site 
    85→                  https://example.com returns HTTP 200. Report any issues found."
    86→    
    87→    Args:
    88→        prompt: Complete, self-contained instructions for the future agent.
    89→                Must include ALL context needed - the agent won't remember anything.
    90→        schedule: When to run. Either:
    91→                  - Duration for one-shot: "30m", "2h", "1d" (runs once)
    92→                  - Interval: "every 30m", "every 2h" (recurring)
    93→                  - Cron expression: "0 9 * * *" (daily at 9am)
    94→                  - ISO timestamp: "2026-02-03T14:00:00" (one-shot at specific time)
    95→        name: Optional human-friendly name for the job (for listing/management)
    96→        repeat: How many times to run. Omit for default behavior:
    97→                - One-shot schedules default to repeat=1 (run once)
    98→                - Intervals/cron default to forever
    99→                - Set repeat=5 to run 5 times then auto-delete
   100→        deliver: Where to send the output. Options:
   101→                 - "origin": Back to where this job was created (default)
   102→                 - "local": Save to local files only (~/.hermes/cron/output/)
   103→                 - "telegram": Send to Telegram home channel
   104→                 - "discord": Send to Discord home channel
   105→                 - "signal": Send to Signal home channel
   106→                 - "telegram:123456": Send to specific chat ID
   107→                 - "signal:+15551234567": Send to specific Signal number
   108→    
   109→    Returns:
   110→        JSON with job_id, next_run time, and confirmation
   111→    """
   112→    # Scan prompt for critical threats before scheduling
   113→    scan_error = _scan_cron_prompt(prompt)
   114→    if scan_error:
   115→        return json.dumps({"success": False, "error": scan_error}, indent=2)
   116→
   117→    # Get origin info from environment if available
   118→    origin = None
   119→    origin_platform = os.getenv("HERMES_SESSION_PLATFORM")
   120→    origin_chat_id = os.getenv("HERMES_SESSION_CHAT_ID")
   121→    if origin_platform and origin_chat_id:
   122→        origin = {
   123→            "platform": origin_platform,
   124→            "chat_id": origin_chat_id,
   125→            "chat_name": os.getenv("HERMES_SESSION_CHAT_NAME"),
   126→        }
   127→    
   128→    try:
   129→        job = create_job(
   130→            prompt=prompt,
   131→            schedule=schedule,
   132→            name=name,
   133→            repeat=repeat,
   134→            deliver=deliver,
   135→            origin=origin
   136→        )
   137→        
   138→        # Format repeat info for display
   139→        times = job["repeat"].get("times")
   140→        if times is None:
   141→            repeat_display = "forever"
   142→        elif times == 1:
   143→            repeat_display = "once"
   144→        else:
   145→            repeat_display = f"{times} times"
   146→        
   147→        return json.dumps({
   148→            "success": True,
   149→            "job_id": job["id"],
   150→            "name": job["name"],
   151→            "schedule": job["schedule_display"],
   152→            "repeat": repeat_display,
   153→            "deliver": job.get("deliver", "local"),
   154→            "next_run_at": job["next_run_at"],
   155→            "message": f"Cronjob '{job['name']}' created. It will run {repeat_display}, deliver to {job.get('deliver', 'local')}, next at {job['next_run_at']}."
   156→        }, indent=2)
   157→        
   158→    except Exception as e:
   159→        return json.dumps({
   160→            "success": False,
   161→            "error": str(e)
   162→        }, indent=2)
   163→
   164→
   165→SCHEDULE_CRONJOB_SCHEMA = {
   166→    "name": "schedule_cronjob",
   167→    "description": """Schedule an automated task to run the agent on a schedule.
   168→
   169→⚠️ CRITICAL: The cronjob runs in a FRESH SESSION with NO CONTEXT from this conversation.
   170→The prompt must be COMPLETELY SELF-CONTAINED with ALL necessary information including:
   171→- Full context and background
   172→- Specific file paths, URLs, server addresses
   173→- Clear instructions and success criteria
   174→- Any credentials or configuration details
   175→
   176→The future agent will NOT remember anything from the current conversation.
   177→
   178→SCHEDULE FORMATS:
   179→- One-shot: "30m", "2h", "1d" (runs once after delay)
   180→- Interval: "every 30m", "every 2h" (recurring)  
   181→- Cron: "0 9 * * *" (cron expression for precise scheduling)
   182→- Timestamp: "2026-02-03T14:00:00" (specific date/time)
   183→
   184→REPEAT BEHAVIOR:
   185→- One-shot schedules: run once by default
   186→- Intervals/cron: run forever by default
   187→- Set repeat=N to run exactly N times then auto-delete
   188→
   189→DELIVERY OPTIONS (where output goes):
   190→- "origin": Back to current chat (default if in messaging platform)
   191→- "local": Save to local files only (default if in CLI)
   192→- "telegram": Send to Telegram home channel
   193→- "discord": Send to Discord home channel
   194→- "telegram:123456": Send to specific chat (if user provides ID)
   195→
   196→NOTE: The agent's final response is auto-delivered to the target — do NOT use
   197→send_message in the prompt. Just have the agent compose its response normally.
   198→
   199→Use for: reminders, periodic checks, scheduled reports, automated maintenance.""",
   200→    "parameters": {

1→"""
     2→Session mirroring for cross-platform message delivery.
     3→
     4→When a message is sent to a platform (via send_message or cron delivery),
     5→this module appends a "delivery-mirror" record to the target session's
     6→transcript so the receiving-side agent has context about what was sent.
     7→
     8→Standalone -- works from CLI, cron, and gateway contexts without needing
     9→the full SessionStore machinery.
    10→"""
    11→
    12→import json
    13→import logging
    14→from datetime import datetime
    15→from pathlib import Path
    16→from typing import Optional
    17→
    18→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    19→
    20→_SESSIONS_DIR = Path.home() / ".hermes" / "sessions"
    21→_SESSIONS_INDEX = _SESSIONS_DIR / "sessions.json"
    22→
    23→
    24→def mirror_to_session(
    25→    platform: str,
    26→    chat_id: str,
    27→    message_text: str,
    28→    source_label: str = "cli",
    29→    thread_id: Optional[str] = None,
    30→) -> bool:
    31→    """
    32→    Append a delivery-mirror message to the target session's transcript.
    33→
    34→    Finds the gateway session that matches the given platform + chat_id,
    35→    then writes a mirror entry to both the JSONL transcript and SQLite DB.
    36→
    37→    Returns True if mirrored successfully, False if no matching session or error.
    38→    All errors are caught -- this is never fatal.
    39→    """
    40→    try:
    41→        session_id = _find_session_id(platform, str(chat_id), thread_id=thread_id)
    42→        if not session_id:
    43→            logger.debug("Mirror: no session found for %s:%s:%s", platform, chat_id, thread_id)
    44→            return False
    45→
    46→        mirror_msg = {
    47→            "role": "assistant",
    48→            "content": message_text,
    49→            "timestamp": datetime.now().isoformat(),
    50→            "mirror": True,
    51→            "mirror_source": source_label,
    52→        }
    53→
    54→        _append_to_jsonl(session_id, mirror_msg)
    55→        _append_to_sqlite(session_id, mirror_msg)
    56→
    57→        logger.debug("Mirror: wrote to session %s (from %s)", session_id, source_label)
    58→        return True
    59→
    60→    except Exception as e:
    61→        logger.debug("Mirror failed for %s:%s:%s: %s", platform, chat_id, thread_id, e)
    62→        return False
    63→
    64→
    65→def _find_session_id(platform: str, chat_id: str, thread_id: Optional[str] = None) -> Optional[str]:
    66→    """
    67→    Find the active session_id for a platform + chat_id pair.
    68→
    69→    Scans sessions.json entries and matches where origin.chat_id == chat_id
    70→    on the right platform.  DM session keys don't embed the chat_id
    71→    (e.g. "agent:main:telegram:dm"), so we check the origin dict.
    72→    """
    73→    if not _SESSIONS_INDEX.exists():
    74→        return None
    75→
    76→    try:
    77→        with open(_SESSIONS_INDEX, encoding="utf-8") as f:
    78→            data = json.load(f)
    79→    except Exception:
    80→        return None
    81→
    82→    platform_lower = platform.lower()
    83→    best_match = None
    84→    best_updated = ""
    85→
    86→    for _key, entry in data.items():
    87→        origin = entry.get("origin") or {}
    88→        entry_platform = (origin.get("platform") or entry.get("platform", "")).lower()
    89→
    90→        if entry_platform != platform_lower:
    91→            continue
    92→
    93→        origin_chat_id = str(origin.get("chat_id", ""))
    94→        if origin_chat_id == str(chat_id):
    95→            origin_thread_id = origin.get("thread_id")
    96→            if thread_id is not None and str(origin_thread_id or "") != str(thread_id):
    97→                continue
    98→            updated = entry.get("updated_at", "")
    99→            if updated > best_updated:
   100→                best_updated = updated
   101→                best_match = entry.get("session_id")
   102→
   103→    return best_match
   104→
   105→
   106→def _append_to_jsonl(session_id: str, message: dict) -> None:
   107→    """Append a message to the JSONL transcript file."""
   108→    transcript_path = _SESSIONS_DIR / f"{session_id}.jsonl"
   109→    try:
   110→        with open(transcript_path, "a", encoding="utf-8") as f:
   111→            f.write(json.dumps(message, ensure_ascii=False) + "\n")
   112→    except Exception as e:
   113→        logger.debug("Mirror JSONL write failed: %s", e)
   114→
   115→
   116→def _append_to_sqlite(session_id: str, message: dict) -> None:
   117→    """Append a message to the SQLite session database."""
   118→    db = None
   119→    try:
   120→        from hermes_state import SessionDB
   121→        db = SessionDB()
   122→        db.append_message(
   123→            session_id=session_id,
   124→            role=message.get("role", "assistant"),
   125→            content=message.get("content"),
   126→        )
   127→    except Exception as e:
   128→        logger.debug("Mirror SQLite write failed: %s", e)
   129→    finally:
   130→        if db is not None:
   131→            db.close()
   132→
```

271→@dataclass
   272→class MessageEvent:
   273→    """
   274→    Incoming message from a platform.
   275→    
   276→    Normalized representation that all adapters produce.
   277→    """
   278→    # Message content
   279→    text: str
   280→    message_type: MessageType = MessageType.TEXT
   281→    
   282→    # Source information
   283→    source: SessionSource = None
   284→    
   285→    # Original platform data
   286→    raw_message: Any = None
   287→    message_id: Optional[str] = None
   288→    
   289→    # Media attachments
   290→    media_urls: List[str] = field(default_factory=list)
   291→    media_types: List[str] = field(default_factory=list)
   292→    
   293→    # Reply context
   294→    reply_to_message_id: Optional[str] = None
   295→    
   296→    # Timestamps
   297→    timestamp: datetime = field(default_factory=datetime.now)
   298→    
   299→    def is_command(self) -> bool:
   300→        """Check if this is a command message (e.g., /new, /reset)."""
   301→        return self.text.startswith("/")
   302→    
   303→    def get_command(self) -> Optional[str]:
   304→        """Extract command name if this is a command message."""
   305→        if not self.is_command():
   306→            return None
   307→        # Split on space and get first word, strip the /
   308→        parts = self.text.split(maxsplit=1)
   309→        return parts[0][1:].lower() if parts else None
   310→    
   311→    def get_command_args(self) -> str:
   312→        """Get the arguments after a command."""
   313→        if not self.is_command():
   314→            return self.text
   315→        parts = self.text.split(maxsplit=1)
   316→        return parts[1] if len(parts) > 1 else ""
   317→
   318→
   319→@dataclass 
   320→class SendResult:
   321→    """Result of sending a message."""
   322→    success: bool
   323→    message_id: Optional[str] = None
   324→    error: Optional[str] = None
   325→    raw_response: Any = None
   326→
   327→
   328→# Type for message handlers
   329→MessageHandler = Callable[[MessageEvent], Awaitable[Optional[str]]]
   330→
   331→
   332→class BasePlatformAdapter(ABC):
   333→    """
   334→    Base class for platform adapters.
   335→    
   336→    Subclasses implement platform-specific logic for:
   337→    - Connecting and authenticating
   338→    - Receiving messages
   339→    - Sending messages/responses
   340→    - Handling media
   341→    """
   342→    
   343→    def __init__(self, config: PlatformConfig, platform: Platform):
   344→        self.config = config
   345→        self.platform = platform
   346→        self._message_handler: Optional[MessageHandler] = None
   347→        self._running = False
   348→        
   349→        # Track active message handlers per session for interrupt support
   350→        # Key: session_key (e.g., chat_id), Value: (event, asyncio.Event for interrupt)
   351→        self._active_sessions: Dict[str, asyncio.Event] = {}
   352→        self._pending_messages: Dict[str, MessageEvent] = {}
   353→    
   354→    @property
   355→    def name(self) -> str:
   356→        """Human-readable name for this adapter."""
   357→        return self.platform.value.title()
   358→    
   359→    @property
   360→    def is_connected(self) -> bool:
   361→        """Check if adapter is currently connected."""
   362→        return self._running
   363→    
   364→    def set_message_handler(self, handler: MessageHandler) -> None:
   365→        """
   366→        Set the handler for incoming messages.
   367→        
   368→        The handler receives a MessageEvent and should return
   369→        an optional response string.
   370→        """
   371→        self._message_handler = handler
   372→    
   373→    @abstractmethod
   374→    async def connect(self) -> bool:
   375→        """
   376→        Connect to the platform and start receiving messages.
   377→        
   378→        Returns True if connection was successful.
   379→        """
   380→        pass
   381→    
   382→    @abstractmethod
   383→    async def disconnect(self) -> None:
   384→        """Disconnect from the platform."""
   385→        pass
   386→    
   387→    @abstractmethod

  1493→            # Save the full conversation to the transcript, including tool calls.
  1494→            # This preserves the complete agent loop (tool_calls, tool results,
  1495→            # intermediate reasoning) so sessions can be resumed with full context
  1496→            # and transcripts are useful for debugging and training data.
  1497→            ts = datetime.now().isoformat()
  1498→            
  1499→            # If this is a fresh session (no history), write the full tool
  1500→            # definitions as the first entry so the transcript is self-describing
  1501→            # -- the same list of dicts sent as tools=[...] in the API request.
  1502→            if not history:
  1503→                tool_defs = agent_result.get("tools", [])
  1504→                self.session_store.append_to_transcript(
  1505→                    session_entry.session_id,
  1506→                    {
  1507→                        "role": "session_meta",
  1508→                        "tools": tool_defs or [],
  1509→                        "model": os.getenv("HERMES_MODEL", ""),
  1510→                        "platform": source.platform.value if source.platform else "",
  1511→                        "timestamp": ts,
  1512→                    }
  1513→                )
  1514→            
  1515→            # Find only the NEW messages from this turn (skip history we loaded).
  1516→            # Use the filtered history length (history_offset) that was actually
  1517→            # passed to the agent, not len(history) which includes session_meta
  1518→            # entries that were stripped before the agent saw them.
  1519→            history_len = agent_result.get("history_offset", len(history))
  1520→            new_messages = agent_messages[history_len:] if len(agent_messages) > history_len else []
  1521→            
  1522→            # If no new messages found (edge case), fall back to simple user/assistant
  1523→            if not new_messages:
  1524→                self.session_store.append_to_transcript(
  1525→                    session_entry.session_id,
  1526→                    {"role": "user", "content": message_text, "timestamp": ts}
  1527→                )
  1528→                if response:
  1529→                    self.session_store.append_to_transcript(
  1530→                        session_entry.session_id,
  1531→                        {"role": "assistant", "content": response, "timestamp": ts}
  1532→                    )
  1533→            else:
  1534→                # The agent already persisted these messages to SQLite via
  1535→                # _flush_messages_to_session_db(), so skip the DB write here
  1536→                # to prevent the duplicate-write bug (#860).  We still write
  1537→                # to JSONL for backward compatibility and as a backup.
  1538→                agent_persisted = self._session_db is not None
  1539→                for msg in new_messages:
  1540→                    # Skip system messages (they're rebuilt each run)
  1541→                    if msg.get("role") == "system":
  1542→                        continue
  1543→                    # Add timestamp to each message for debugging
  1544→                    entry = {**msg, "timestamp": ts}
  1545→                    self.session_store.append_to_transcript(
  1546→                        session_entry.session_id, entry,
  1547→                        skip_db=agent_persisted,
  1548→                    )
  1549→            
  1550→            # Update session with actual prompt token count from the agent
  1551→            self.session_store.update_session(
  1552→                session_entry.session_key,
  1553→                last_prompt_tokens=agent_result.get("last_prompt_tokens", 0),
  1554→            )
  1555→            
  1556→            return response
  1557→            
  1558→        except Exception:
  1559→            logger.exception("Agent error in session %s", session_key)
  1560→            return (
  1561→                "Sorry, I encountered an unexpected error. "
  1562→                "The details have been logged for debugging. "
  1563→                "Try again or use /reset to start a fresh session."
  1564→            )
  1565→        finally:
  1566→            # Clear session env
  1567→            self._clear_session_env()

   894→        """

   896→

   904→    def truncate_message(self, content: str, max_length: int = 4096) -> List[str]:
   905→        """
   906→        Split a long message into chunks, preserving code block boundaries.
   907→
   908→        When a split falls inside a triple-backtick code block, the fence is
   909→        closed at the end of the current chunk and reopened (with the original
   910→        language tag) at the start of the next chunk.  Multi-chunk responses
   911→        receive indicators like ``(1/3)``.
   912→
   913→        Args:
   914→            content: The full message content
   915→            max_length: Maximum length per chunk (platform-specific)
   916→
   917→        Returns:
   918→            List of message chunks
   919→        """
   920→        if len(content) <= max_length:
   921→            return [content]
   922→
   923→        INDICATOR_RESERVE = 10   # room for " (XX/XX)"
   924→        FENCE_CLOSE = "\n```"
   925→
   926→        chunks: List[str] = []
   927→        remaining = content
   928→        # When the previous chunk ended mid-code-block, this holds the
   929→        # language tag (possibly "") so we can reopen the fence.
   930→        carry_lang: Optional[str] = None
   931→
   932→        while remaining:
   933→            # If we're continuing a code block from the previous chunk,
   934→            # prepend a new opening fence with the same language tag.
   935→            prefix = f"```{carry_lang}\n" if carry_lang is not None else ""
   936→
   937→            # How much body text we can fit after accounting for the prefix,
   938→            # a potential closing fence, and the chunk indicator.
   939→            headroom = max_length - INDICATOR_RESERVE - len(prefix) - len(FENCE_CLOSE)
   940→            if headroom < 1:
   941→                headroom = max_length // 2
   942→
   943→            # Everything remaining fits in one final chunk
   944→            if len(prefix) + len(remaining) <= max_length - INDICATOR_RESERVE:
   945→                chunks.append(prefix + remaining)
   946→                break
   947→
   948→            # Find a natural split point (prefer newlines, then spaces)
   949→            region = remaining[:headroom]
   950→            split_at = region.rfind("\n")
   951→            if split_at < headroom // 2:
   952→                split_at = region.rfind(" ")
   953→            if split_at < 1:
   954→                split_at = headroom
   955→
   956→            chunk_body = remaining[:split_at]
   957→            remaining = remaining[split_at:].lstrip()
   958→
   959→            full_chunk = prefix + chunk_body
   960→
   961→            # Walk only the chunk_body (not the prefix we prepended) to
   962→            # determine whether we end inside an open code block.
   963→            in_code = carry_lang is not None
   964→            lang = carry_lang or ""
   965→            for line in chunk_body.split("\n"):
   966→                stripped = line.strip()
   967→                if stripped.startswith("```"):
   968→                    if in_code:
   969→                        in_code = False
   970→                        lang = ""
   971→                    else:
   972→                        in_code = True
   973→                        tag = stripped[3:].strip()
   974→                        lang = tag.split()[0] if tag else ""
   975→
   976→            if in_code:
   977→                # Close the orphaned fence so the chunk is valid on its own
   978→                full_chunk += FENCE_CLOSE
   979→                carry_lang = lang
   980→            else:
   981→                carry_lang = None
   982→
   983→            chunks.append(full_chunk)
   984→
   985→        # Append chunk indicators when the response spans multiple messages
   986→        if len(chunks) > 1:
   987→            total = len(chunks)
   988→            chunks = [
   989→                f"{chunk} ({i + 1}/{total})" for i, chunk in enumerate(chunks)
   990→            ]
   991→
   992→        return chunks
   993→

Now I have all the information I need. Let me create a comprehensive summary document with all my findings:

## Summary: Auto-Reply Feature Implementation for Hermes Agent

Based on my thorough exploration of the codebase, here's what I found about how to implement an auto-reply feature:

### 1. **Message Handler Return & Response Flow** (`gateway/platforms/base.py` lines 694-827)

**Key Finding:** The `_handle_message` returns a string response that gets sent back through the platform adapter.

**Flow:**
- `_handle_message(event)` → returns `Optional[str]`
- Response is extracted and sent via `adapter.send(chat_id, content)` (lines 722-728)
- Media (images, audio) are sent separately after the text response (lines 743-812)
- **After everything is sent**, the code checks for **pending messages** (line 815-827):
  ```python
  if session_key in self._pending_messages:
      pending_event = self._pending_messages.pop(session_key)
      # ... process pending message in new background task
      await self._process_message_background(pending_event, session_key)
  ```

**This is the KEY MECHANISM for injection!** The system already supports queuing synthetic messages via `_pending_messages[session_key]`, which get processed after the agent completes.

---

### 2. **Session State Storage** (`gateway/session.py` lines 218-295)

**Session Metadata Stored:**
- `SessionEntry` dataclass (lines 218-295) stores per-session metadata:
  - `session_key`: deterministic routing key
  - `session_id`: unique session identifier
  - `created_at`, `updated_at`: timestamps
  - `origin`: SessionSource (platform, chat_id, user_id, etc.)
  - `display_name`: human-readable name
  - `platform`: Platform enum
  - `chat_type`: "dm", "group", "channel"
  - `input_tokens`, `output_tokens`: token tracking
  - **Custom metadata can be added here** - the class is mutable and serializes to/from dict (lines 250-294)

**Session Storage Methods:**
- `update_session()` (lines 566-593): Updates token counts and metadata
- `_save()` (lines 371-392): Persists to `~/.hermes/sessions/sessions.json`
- Sessions.json uses `session_key` → `SessionEntry` mapping (line 377)

You could extend `SessionEntry` with an `auto_reply_config` field containing:
- `enabled`: bool
- `delay_seconds`: int
- `message_template`: str
- `trigger_patterns`: list[str]

**Pattern for Auto-Reply:**

### 4. **Synthetic Message Injection Mechanism** (KEY DISCOVERY)

**The cleanest injection point is the INTERRUPT PATTERN (`gateway/platforms/base.py` lines 814-827 and 662-668):**

**Current Mechanism:**
- When a new message arrives while agent is running, it goes into `_pending_messages[session_key]` (line 665)
- After agent completes, pending message is retrieved and reprocessed (line 816)
- Creates a new `MessageEvent` and calls `_process_message_background()` (line 827)

**For Auto-Reply, you could:**

**Option A: Inject via _pending_messages (Minimal Changes)**
```python
# In _handle_message or a new method
if should_send_auto_reply(source, session_key):
    synthetic_event = MessageEvent(
        text=auto_reply_message,
        message_type=MessageType.TEXT,
        source=source,
        raw_message=None,
        timestamp=datetime.now()
    )
    adapter._pending_messages[session_key] = synthetic_event
```

The existing code at line 815-827 will automatically process it!

**Option B: Inject directly in _handle_message (Most Control)**
```python
# After agent response is sent (line 1556)
if should_queue_auto_reply(source, session_key, response):
    auto_reply_text = generate_auto_reply(response, config)
    # Create synthetic event
    auto_event = MessageEvent(...)
    # Could queue it or process immediately
    await self._handle_message(auto_event)  # Recursive call
```

### 5. **No Existing Cron/Scheduled Message Patterns Found**

I searched `/tools/` directory - the cron system (`cronjob_tools.py`) schedules **agents** to run prompts, not messages to be sent. The `send_message_tool.py` is for immediate delivery, not scheduling.

**Auto-Reply would be the first per-session delayed/conditional messaging feature.**

For synthetic injection, create `MessageEvent` with:
```python
@dataclass
class MessageEvent:
    text: str                                  # The message
    message_type: MessageType = TEXT           # TEXT, PHOTO, VOICE, etc.
    source: SessionSource = None               # Platform, chat_id, user_id
    raw_message: Any = None                    # Can be None for synthetic
    message_id: Optional[str] = None           # Can be None
    media_urls: List[str] = []                 # Media attachments
    media_types: List[str] = []                # MIME types
    reply_to_message_id: Optional[str] = None  # For threading
    timestamp: datetime = now()                # Can be current time
```

**Minimal synthetic event:**
```python
MessageEvent(
    text="auto-reply message",
    message_type=MessageType.TEXT,
    source=event.source,  # Reuse original source
    raw_message=None,     # Not a real platform message
    timestamp=datetime.now()
)
```

### 9. **Exact Code Path Example: How to Inject a Message**

```python
# In _handle_message or new helper method around line 1556

# After agent response is sent and processed...
if auto_reply_should_trigger(source, response, session_key):
    auto_reply_text = get_auto_reply_text(session_key)
    
    # Option 1: Queue for processing after current message completes
    synthetic_event = MessageEvent(
        text=auto_reply_text,
        message_type=MessageType.TEXT,
        source=source,  # Same chat/user
        raw_message=None,
        timestamp=datetime.now()
    )
    # Adapter will process this automatically after cleanup
    if session_key in self.adapters[source.platform]._active_sessions:
        self.adapters[source.platform]._pending_messages[session_key] = synthetic_event
    
    # Option 2: Send directly without re-queuing through agent
    adapter = self.adapters.get(source.platform)
    if adapter:
        await adapter.send(
            chat_id=source.chat_id,
            content=auto_reply_text,
            metadata={"thread_id": source.thread_id} if source.thread_id else None
        )
```

> DEVELOPER

Design an implementation plan for adding an "auto-reply" feature to the Hermes agent. Here's the context:

## What the user wants
When the agent responds and it's the "user's turn" to reply, instead of waiting for a real user message, the system should:
1. Take the conversation context
2. Send it to an LLM with user-provided instructions (a "meta-prompt" for how to auto-reply)
3. Generate a reply
4. Post that reply as if the user sent it (so the agent processes it normally)

User instructions should be prioritized over context in the auto-reply generation.

## Architecture Context

### Key files and flow:
- **`gateway/platforms/base.py`**: Platform adapters handle message I/O
  - `handle_message(event)` (line 648): Entry point, spawns background task
  - `_process_message_background(event, session_key)` (line 694): Processes message, calls `_message_handler` (which is `GatewayRunner._handle_message`), sends response, then checks `self._pending_messages` for queued messages
  - `_pending_messages: Dict[str, MessageEvent]` (line 352): Queues messages during active sessions, auto-processed after response sent (line 815-828)
  - `_active_sessions: Dict[str, asyncio.Event]` (line 348): Tracks which sessions are currently running

- **`gateway/run.py`**: GatewayRunner orchestrates everything
  - `_handle_message(event)` (line 867): Core pipeline - auth, commands, session management, runs agent, saves transcript, returns response string
  - `_run_agent(message, context_prompt, history, source, session_id, session_key)` (line 3028): Runs AIAgent in thread pool
  - Commands pattern: `/personality`, `/model` etc. stored as `self._ephemeral_*` dicts
  - `self._running_agents: Dict[str, Any]` tracks active agents per session
  - `self._pending_messages: Dict[str, str]` for interrupt text queueing (separate from adapter's)
  - After agent returns, response is returned from `_handle_message` → adapter sends it → adapter checks its own `_pending_messages`

- **`run_agent.py`**: AIAgent class with conversation loop
  - Uses OpenAI SDK client (via OpenRouter) for LLM calls
  - `run_conversation(message)` does the tool-calling loop

- **`gateway/session.py`**: Session storage, `SessionEntry` dataclass, `SessionStore`

### Message injection pattern:
The adapter's `_pending_messages` dict is the natural injection point. After a response is sent (base.py line 814-828), it checks this dict and if there's a pending `MessageEvent`, it calls `_process_message_background` recursively. This is perfect for auto-reply - we just need to queue a synthetic MessageEvent there.

However, the adapter's `_pending_messages` and `_active_sessions` are on the adapter instance, and `_handle_message` is in GatewayRunner. GatewayRunner has access to adapters via `self.adapters: Dict[Platform, BasePlatformAdapter]`.

### LLM call pattern:
The existing `AIAgent` uses OpenAI SDK with OpenRouter. For auto-reply generation, we need a lightweight LLM call (no tools, just text generation). We can either:
- Create a minimal AIAgent instance
- Make a direct OpenAI client call (simpler, less overhead)

## Design Requirements
1. `/autoreply` command to set/clear the auto-reply meta-prompt
2. Storage of auto-reply config per session (or global)
3. LLM call to generate the auto-reply using conversation context + user instructions
4. Injection of generated reply as a synthetic user message
5. Safety: max auto-reply iterations to prevent infinite loops
6. User instructions should be prioritized (system prompt, not just context)

## Design Considerations
- The auto-reply LLM call should be lightweight - just a simple completion, no tools
- We need to extract recent conversation context to feed to the auto-reply LLM
- The synthetic message should flow through the normal pipeline so it gets full agent treatment
- Need to handle the case where auto-reply is disabled mid-conversation
- Should we use the same model or allow a different (cheaper) model for auto-replies?

Please provide a detailed implementation plan covering:
1. Where to store auto-reply config
2. The `/autoreply` command handler
3. The auto-reply generation logic (LLM call)
4. The injection mechanism
5. Safety guards
6. Which files to modify and key code changes

   867→    async def _handle_message(self, event: MessageEvent) -> Optional[str]:
   868→        """
   869→        Handle an incoming message from any platform.
   870→        
   871→        This is the core message processing pipeline:
   872→        1. Check user authorization
   873→        2. Check for commands (/new, /reset, etc.)
   874→        3. Check for running agent and interrupt if needed
   875→        4. Get or create session
   876→        5. Build context for agent
   877→        6. Run agent conversation
   878→        7. Return response
   879→        """
   880→        source = event.source
   881→        
   882→        # Check if user is authorized
   883→        if not self._is_user_authorized(source):
   884→            logger.warning("Unauthorized user: %s (%s) on %s", source.user_id, source.user_name, source.platform.value)
   885→            # In DMs: offer pairing code. In groups: silently ignore.
   886→            if source.chat_type == "dm":
   887→                platform_name = source.platform.value if source.platform else "unknown"
   888→                code = self.pairing_store.generate_code(
   889→                    platform_name, source.user_id, source.user_name or ""
   890→                )
   891→                if code:
   892→                    adapter = self.adapters.get(source.platform)
   893→                    if adapter:
   894→                        await adapter.send(
   895→                            source.chat_id,
   896→                            f"Hi~ I don't recognize you yet!\n\n"
   897→                            f"Here's your pairing code: `{code}`\n\n"
   898→                            f"Ask the bot owner to run:\n"
   899→                            f"`hermes pairing approve {platform_name} {code}`"
   900→                        )
   901→                else:
   902→                    adapter = self.adapters.get(source.platform)
   903→                    if adapter:
   904→                        await adapter.send(
   905→                            source.chat_id,
   906→                            "Too many pairing requests right now~ "
   907→                            "Please try again later!"
   908→                        )
   909→            return None
   910→        
   911→        # PRIORITY: If an agent is already running for this session, interrupt it
   912→        # immediately. This is before command parsing to minimize latency -- the
   913→        # user's "stop" message reaches the agent as fast as possible.
   914→        [REDACTED](source)
   915→        if _quick_key in self._running_agents:
   916→            running_agent = self._running_agents[_quick_key]
   917→            logger.debug("PRIORITY interrupt for session %s", _quick_key[:20])
   918→            running_agent.interrupt(event.text)
   919→            if _quick_key in self._pending_messages:
   920→                self._pending_messages[_quick_key] += "\n" + event.text
   921→            else:
   922→                self._pending_messages[_quick_key] = event.text
   923→            return None
