Now I have a complete picture. Here is the implementation plan.

---

## Auto-Reply Feature Implementation Plan

### Overview

The auto-reply feature generates synthetic user messages using an LLM after the agent responds, creating an automated conversation loop. The feature is controlled per-session via a `/autoreply` command that sets a meta-prompt instructing the LLM how to reply.

### 1. Storage: Auto-Reply Config

**Where:** In-memory on `GatewayRunner`, keyed by session_key. No persistence needed (ephemeral like `/personality`).

**Add to `GatewayRunner.__init__` (around line 248 of `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/gateway/run.py`):**

```python
# Auto-reply config per session
# Key: session_key, Value: {"prompt": str, "model": str|None, "max_iterations": int, "iteration_count": int}
self._autoreply_configs: Dict[str, Dict[str, Any]] = {}
```

The dict stores:
- `prompt` -- the user's meta-prompt (instructions for how to auto-reply)
- `model` -- optional override model for the auto-reply LLM call (defaults to a cheap model like `openai/gpt-4o-mini`)
- `max_iterations` -- safety cap, default 10
- `iteration_count` -- counter reset each time a real user message arrives

### 2. The `/autoreply` Command Handler

**Where:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/gateway/run.py`

**Step 2a:** Add `"autoreply"` to `_known_commands` set at line 929.

**Step 2b:** Add command dispatch at line ~1000 (alongside other command handlers):
```python
if command == "autoreply":
    return await self._handle_autoreply_command(event)
```

**Step 2c:** Implement `_handle_autoreply_command` as a new method on `GatewayRunner`. Follow the pattern of `_handle_personality_command` (line 1873). Behavior:

- `/autoreply` (no args) -- show current status + usage
- `/autoreply <prompt>` -- enable auto-reply with the given meta-prompt
- `/autoreply off` or `/autoreply stop` -- disable auto-reply for this session
- `/autoreply max <N>` -- set max iteration count
- `/autoreply model <model>` -- set which model to use for auto-reply generation

The handler builds the session_key from `event.source`, then sets/clears `self._autoreply_configs[session_key]`.

**Step 2d:** Add to `/help` output (around line 1650) so users discover the command.

### 3. Auto-Reply Generation Logic (LLM Call)

**Where:** New method `_generate_autoreply` on `GatewayRunner` in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/gateway/run.py`.

This is the core logic. It needs to:

1. **Extract recent conversation context** from the transcript. Load the last N messages (e.g., last 20 user/assistant messages) from the session transcript via `self.session_store.load_transcript(session_id)`. Filter to only `role in ("user", "assistant")` entries with `content` (skip tool calls, system, session_meta).

2. **Build the LLM request.** Use the OpenAI SDK directly (not AIAgent -- no tools needed). The call structure:

```python
async def _generate_autoreply(self, session_key: str, session_id: str) -> Optional[str]:
    config = self._autoreply_configs.get(session_key)
    if not config:
        return None
    
    # Safety check
    if config["iteration_count"] >= config["max_iterations"]:
        del self._autoreply_configs[session_key]
        return None  # or return a notification message
    
    # Load recent history
    history = self.session_store.load_transcript(session_id)
    recent = [m for m in (history or []) if m.get("role") in ("user", "assistant") and m.get("content")][-20:]
    
    # Build messages for the auto-reply LLM
    messages = [
        {"role": "system", "content": (
            "You are generating a reply on behalf of the user in a conversation with an AI assistant. "
            "The user has given you the following instructions for how to reply. "
            "These instructions are your TOP PRIORITY -- follow them precisely.\n\n"
            f"USER INSTRUCTIONS:\n{config['prompt']}\n\n"
            "Generate ONLY the user's reply message. Do not include any meta-commentary, "
            "do not prefix with 'User:' or similar labels. Just output the raw message text."
        )}
    ]
    # Add conversation context (re-labeled so the LLM sees it as context)
    for m in recent:
        if m["role"] == "assistant":
            messages.append({"role": "assistant", "content": f"[AI Assistant said]: {m['content']}"})
        else:
            messages.append({"role": "user", "content": f"[Previous user message]: {m['content']}"})
    
    messages.append({"role": "user", "content": "Now generate the next user reply based on your instructions."})
    
    # Make the LLM call
    from openai import OpenAI
    model = config.get("model") or "openai/gpt-4o-mini"
    client = OpenAI(
        api_key=os.getenv("OPENROUTER_API_KEY"),
        base_url="https://openrouter.ai/api/v1",
    )
    
    loop = asyncio.get_event_loop()
    response = await loop.run_in_executor(None, lambda: client.chat.completions.create(
        model=model,
        messages=messages,
        max_tokens=1024,
        temperature=0.7,
    ))
    
    reply_text = response.choices[0].message.content.strip()
    config["iteration_count"] += 1
    return reply_text
```

Key design decisions:
- **System prompt prioritizes user instructions** -- the meta-prompt is embedded in the system message as the top priority, not just appended as context.
- **Uses a cheap model by default** (gpt-4o-mini) to keep costs low.
- **Direct OpenAI client call** -- no AIAgent overhead, no tools, no streaming. Simple and fast.
- **Runs in executor** since the OpenAI SDK call is synchronous.

### 4. Injection Mechanism

This is the most architecturally important part. There are two viable approaches:

**Chosen approach: Inject in `_handle_message` after agent returns, before returning to adapter.**

**Where:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/gateway/run.py`, at the end of `_handle_message`, around line 1556 (just before `return response`).

After the agent completes and the transcript is saved, but before returning the response string to the adapter, we check if auto-reply is enabled and generate the next message. Instead of returning just the response, we queue the auto-reply into the adapter's `_pending_messages` dict so it processes naturally after the response is sent.

**Modified flow (at end of `_handle_message`, around line 1556):**

```python
# --- Auto-reply injection ---
if session_key in self._autoreply_configs:
    try:
        autoreply_text = await self._generate_autoreply(session_key, session_entry.session_id)
        if autoreply_text:
            # Find the adapter for this platform
            adapter = self.adapters.get(source.platform)
            if adapter:
                # Construct a synthetic MessageEvent
                synthetic_event = MessageEvent(
                    text=autoreply_text,
                    source=source,  # same source as original
                    message_id=f"autoreply-{datetime.now().timestamp()}",
                )
                # Queue it in the adapter's _pending_messages
                [REDACTED](source)
                adapter._pending_messages[adapter_session_key] = synthetic_event
                logger.info("[AutoReply] Queued auto-reply for %s: %s", session_key[:20], autoreply_text[:100])
    except Exception as e:
        logger.error("[AutoReply] Failed to generate auto-reply: %s", e)

return response
```

**Why this approach works:**
- The adapter's `_process_message_background` (base.py line 814-828) already checks `self._pending_messages` after sending the response and recursively processes any queued message. This is the exact pattern used for interrupt message queueing.
- The synthetic event flows through the entire normal pipeline: `_process_message_background` -> `_message_handler` (which is `_handle_message`) -> auth check, session load, agent run, transcript save, etc.
- The auto-reply message will appear in the transcript as a normal user message.
- The next iteration will also check for auto-reply at the end, creating the loop naturally.

**Critical timing detail:** The auto-reply LLM call happens while `_active_sessions[session_key]` is still set (we are still inside `_process_message_background`). This is fine because we are injecting into `_pending_messages`, not trying to process immediately. The adapter will pick it up after cleanup.

### 5. Safety Guards

**5a. Max iteration cap (primary guard):**
- Default `max_iterations = 10` per auto-reply activation.
- `iteration_count` increments each auto-reply. When exhausted, auto-reply config is removed and a notification is sent.
- When the cap is hit, instead of silently stopping, append a status message to the response: `"\n\n_[Auto-reply limit reached (10/10). Use /autoreply to re-enable.]_"`

**5b. Real user message resets counter:**
- In `_handle_message`, after command parsing but before agent execution (around line 1068), check if the incoming message is NOT a synthetic auto-reply. If it is a real user message and auto-reply is active, reset `iteration_count = 0`. This way a real user interjecting refreshes the cap.
- Mark synthetic events: add a flag `is_autoreply: bool = False` to `MessageEvent` (default False, set True on synthetic events). Or simpler: check `message_id.startswith("autoreply-")`.

**5c. `/autoreply off` stops immediately:**
- Removes the config from `_autoreply_configs`. 
- Even if an auto-reply is already queued in `_pending_messages`, it will still flow through `_handle_message` which checks `_autoreply_configs` before generating the NEXT auto-reply. So the chain stops after at most one more iteration.

**5d. `/stop` and `/reset` clear auto-reply:**
- Add cleanup to `_handle_reset_command` (line 1569) and `_handle_stop_command`:
  ```python
  self._autoreply_configs.pop(session_key, None)
  ```

**5e. Error handling:**
- If the auto-reply LLM call fails, log the error and do NOT queue anything. The chain stops naturally.
- Wrap the entire auto-reply block in try/except so it never breaks the normal response flow.

### 6. Files to Modify and Key Changes

| File | Changes |
|------|---------|
| `gateway/run.py` | Add `_autoreply_configs` dict to `__init__`; add `/autoreply` to `_known_commands`; add command dispatch; implement `_handle_autoreply_command`; implement `_generate_autoreply`; add injection logic at end of `_handle_message`; add cleanup to `/reset` and `/stop` handlers; add to `/help` output |
| `gateway/platforms/base.py` | Potentially add `is_autoreply` field to `MessageEvent` dataclass (line 272) for clean identification of synthetic messages. Minimal change. |

**No other files need modification.** The feature is self-contained within the gateway layer.

### 7. Sequencing / Implementation Order

1. Add `is_autoreply` field to `MessageEvent` in `base.py` (1 line)
2. Add `_autoreply_configs` dict to `GatewayRunner.__init__` in `run.py`
3. Implement `_handle_autoreply_command` method
4. Wire up command dispatch (add to `_known_commands`, add `if command == "autoreply"` block)
5. Implement `_generate_autoreply` method
6. Add injection logic at end of `_handle_message` (before `return response` on line 1556)
7. Add cleanup to `_handle_reset_command` and `_handle_stop_command`
8. Add to `/help` output
9. Test the loop: `/autoreply say "continue"` then send a message, verify the auto-reply chain fires and stops at the cap.

### 8. Potential Challenges

- **Adapter access from GatewayRunner:** `_handle_message` needs to write to `adapter._pending_messages`. GatewayRunner has `self.adapters: Dict[Platform, BasePlatformAdapter]`, and the source event carries `source.platform`, so `self.adapters[source.platform]._pending_messages` is accessible. This is a slight abstraction violation (reaching into adapter internals), but follows the same pattern already used elsewhere in the codebase.

- **Race condition with real user messages:** If a real user sends a message while the auto-reply LLM call is in progress, the adapter's interrupt mechanism handles this correctly -- the real message goes to `_pending_messages`, overwriting the not-yet-queued auto-reply. This is correct behavior (real user input takes priority).

- **Context window for auto-reply LLM:** Using only the last 20 user/assistant messages keeps token usage bounded. For very long conversations, this is sufficient context for the auto-reply LLM to understand what to say next.

- **Model availability:** The auto-reply model (default gpt-4o-mini) must be available via the configured API key. Using OpenRouter ensures broad model access.

### Critical Files for Implementation
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/gateway/run.py` - Core implementation: command handler, auto-reply generation, injection logic, all in GatewayRunner
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/gateway/platforms/base.py` - MessageEvent dataclass (add is_autoreply field) and _pending_messages injection target (lines 814-828)
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/gateway/session.py` - SessionSource/build_session_key used for constructing synthetic events and session key lookups
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/run_agent.py` - Reference for OpenAI client initialization pattern to reuse for the lightweight auto-reply LLM call
